PREFACE 
 
 I, the Chairman of the Department-related Parliamentary Standing Committee on Home 
Affairs, having been authorised by the Committee to submit the report on its behalf, do hereby prese nt 
this Two Hundred Thirty Second Report on Demands for Grants (2021-22) relating to the Ministry of 
Development of North Eastern Region (DoNER). 
 
2. Under Rule 272  of the Rules of Procedure and Conduct of Business in the Council of States, 
the Department-related Parliamentary Standing Committee on Home Affairs is mandated to consider 
the Demands for Grants of the related Ministries and make report thereon. In pursuance thereof, the 
Committee, in its sitting held on 23 rd February, 2021 heard the presentation of the Secretary and other 
officials of the Ministry of DoNER as well as officers of the implementing 
Ministries/Departments/Agencies and considered the Demands for Grants (2021-22) of that Ministry.  
 
3. The Committee, in its sitting held on 10 th March, 2021
 considered and adopted the draft 
Report. 
 
4. The Committee, while making its observations/recommendations, has relied mainly upon the 
following papers received from the Ministry of DoNER: - 
 
(i) Annual Report (2020-21) of the Ministry; 
(ii) Detailed Demands for Grants (2021-22) pertaining to the Ministry of DoNER; 
(iii) Background Notes on the Demands for Grants (2021-22); 
(iv) Written replies furnished by the Ministry to the Questionnaires sent to it by the 
Committee Secretariat; 
(v) Presentation made by the Secretary of the Ministry; and 
(vi) Replies to the queries/comments/suggestions of the Members, raised during the 
Committee's meetings, as furnished by the Departments/Ministries and other 
stakeholders during various meetings of the Committee; and 
(vii) Verbatim transcripts of the meetings of the Committee held on 23rd February, 2021. 
 
5. For facility of reference and convenience, the observations and recommendations of the 
Committee have been printed in bold letters in the body of the Report. 
 
 
10th  March, 2021

CHAPTER-I 
 
OVERVIEW 
 
1.1 Introduction 
1.1.1 North Eastern Region (NER) comprises eight States namely Arunachal Pradesh, Assam, 
Manipur, Meghalaya, Mizoram, Nagaland, Sikkim and Tripura with a total area of 2,62,179 Sq. 
km. This sums up to 118 districts and 7.98% of the total geographical area of the country. As per 
Census 2011, the population of the region is about 45 million (4.5 crore), constituting 3.78% of 
the total population of India. The rural population contributes 81.64% of the total population of 
the region, while the urban population is 18.36%. The Scheduled Castes (SC) comprises 6.62% 
of the population of the region whereas Scheduled Tribes (ST) is 27.70% of the population. As 
per Census 2011, the population density of the region is 175 persons per sq. km. varying from 17 
in Arunachal Pradesh to 398 in Assam. In terms of the literacy rate (75%), the sex ratio (959) and 
child sex ratio (960) of NE States stands above the national average.  The region shares 
International Borders of about 5483 km with several neighboring countries- Nepal (99 km), 
Bhutan (516 km), China (Tibet) (1346 km), Myanmar (1643 km) and Bangladesh (1879 km). As 
per the Forest Survey of India, 2019, approximately 65.05% of the region is covered by forests  
and trees, out of this an area of 53,538 Sq. Km in the region is bamboo bearing, which is almost 
33.45% of the total bamboo bearing area of the country. The area is characterized by its rich bio-
diversity and heavy precipitation and has immense potential for development.   
 
1.1.2 To give focused attention to address the special needs of the NE Region, the Department 
of Development of North Eastern Region (DoNER) was set up in 2001 and converted into a full-
fledged Ministry in 2004. It is the only Ministry with territorial jurisdiction. Its vision is to 
accelerate the pace of socio-economic development of the NER so that it may enjoy growth 
parity with the rest of the country. For this, it coordinates with various Ministries/Departments 
primarily concerned with development activities in NER. However, respective 
Ministries/Departments remain responsible for the implementation of programmes in their 
respective fields.  
1.2 Mandate and Responsibilities of the Ministry of DoNER 
1.2.1 As per the Government of India (Allocation of Business) Rules, 1961 the Second 
Schedule (Rule 3), the subjects allotted to the Ministry of DoNER are as follows:- 
(i) Matters relating to the planning, execution and monitoring of developmental 
schemes and projects of North Eastern Region including those in the sectors of 
Power, Irrigation, Roads and Communications. 
  (ii) Hill Area Development Programme in North Eastern Region. 
 (iii) Non-lapsable Fund for the North Eastern Region. 
 (iv) North Eastern Council. 
 (v) North East Development Finance Institution (NEDFi). 
(vi) North Eastern Regional Agricultural Marketing Corporation Limited 
(NERAMAC). 
 (vii) The Sikkim Mining Corporation Limited. 
 (viii) North Eastern Handloom and Handicrafts Development Corporation 
  (NEHHDC), Shillong. 

2 
 
(ix) Road works financed in whole or in part by the Central Government in the NER. 
(x) Planning of road and inland waterways transport in the NER. 
 
1.3 Coordination with Central Ministries and Departments   
 
1.3.1 All the Central Ministries/ Departments (except those specifically exempted by Ministry 
of DoNER) are required to spend 10% of Gross Budgetary Support (GBS) from their allocation 
under Central Sector Schemes and under Centrally Sponsored Schemes for the benefit of North 
Eastern Region. Further, funds for the NER are being allocated annually on a lump sum basis by 
the non-exempted Central Ministries/Departments at the BE/RE stage, under the Major Heads 
2552 (for revenue expenditure), 4552 (for capital expenditure) and 6552 (for loan expenditure). 
These funds are expended under their respective functional schemes. These schemes may either 
be in the form of direct intervention by the Ministries/Departments or through their 
institutions/agencies or may even be through the concerned State Governments. 
 
1.3.2 The Ministry of DoNER, being the nodal Ministry, is mandated to coordinate with 
various Ministries/Departments primarily concerned with development and welfare activities in 
the NER and keeps track of expenditure under the mandatory 10 percent GBS by the non-
exempted Ministries/ Departments since its inception. This exercise is being undertaken on an 
annual basis and the allocation and actual expenditure figures by the respective Ministries/ 
Departments are collected, compiled and forwarded annually to the Ministry of Finance for 
vetting. A total of Rs. 51270.90 crore was allocated by 54 Central Ministries/Departments for 
NE Region during 2020-21 (RE), including the Ministry of DoNER. Similarly, for the Financial 
Year 2021-22, the total budgetary allocation for the NER by 54 non-exempted 
Ministries/Departments is Rs.  68020.24 crore. The Annual Expenditure pertaining to the 
earmarked allocation by the non-exempted Ministries/ Departments from 2017-18 to 2021-22 is 
given in the following table:  
Table 1.1

1.3.3 The Ministry of DoNER has been delegated the powers to review the quarterly 
expenditure of the non-exempted Ministries and recommend reallocation of likely surrendered 
amount to those Ministries/Departments, which have the absorptive capacity to implement 
additional approved schemes/programmes within the financial year. This is a step towards fuller 
utilization of allocation of resources for the North Eastern Region. 

3 
 
1.4 Demand No. 22 and Schemes Being implemented thereunder 
 
1.4.1 Demand No. 22, in the List of Demands for Grants 2021-22 pertains to the Ministry of 
DoNER and the budget of the Ministry includes the following schemes under various major-
heads: 
(i) Major Head 2052 (Secretariat General Services): Secretariat General Services 
covers the salaries and other administrative expenses of the Ministry of DoNER.  
(ii)  Major Head 2070 (Other Administrative Services), North Eastern Council,

Council, Shillong.

(NLCPR)-Central; 
(c) North East Road Sector Development Scheme-Programme Component; 
and

Development Corporation Limited (NEHHDC) 
 
(vii)  Major Head 6885 (Other loans to Industries & Minerals): Loan to the North 
Eastern Development Finance Corporation Limited (NEDFI) 
 
 
 

4 
 
1.5 Central Pool of Resources for NER 
 
1.5.1 In 1997-98, when it was found that the actual expenditure of Ministries/Departments for 
NER was less than the mandatory ceiling of 10 percent, the Government took the decision to 
create a "Central Resource Pool" into which the unutilized portion of the 10 percent earmarked  
GBS would accrue at the end of the financial year.  Since 1998-99, the annual surrendered 
amounts out of the earmarked outlays of all the non-exempted Central Ministries/Departments 
for NER have been accounted as accruals to this Pool and the annual expenditure of the Ministry 
under the NLCPR scheme is being accounted as withdrawal from the Pool. However, this is a 
notional pool, maintained on a proforma basis by the Ministry of Finance. The Ministry of 
DoNER monitors Ministry-wise expenditure/ surrendered amounts for the purpose of 
ascertaining the accrual to the NLCPR pool in consultation with the Ministry of Finance. 
 
INSTITUTIONS UNDER MINISTRY OF DONER 
 
1.6 North Eastern Council (NEC) 
 
1.6.1 The NEC was constituted as a statutory advisory body under the NEC Act 1971 (84 of 
1971) for rapid socio-economic development of the North Eastern Region (NER) under the 
administrative control of the Ministry of DoNER. It is a statutory Regional Planning Body, 
which provides financial assistance for a variety of projects including infrastructure projects like 
construction of roads, improving air connectivity, etc., through State Governments.  
 
1.7 North East Cane and Bamboo Development Council (NECBDC) 
 
1.7.1  Cane and Bamboo Technology Centre (CBTC) was registered as a Society on 21 st July, 
2004 with Secretary NEC as Chairman. It works for the development of the cane and bamboo 
sector in the country, especially in the NE Region. CBTC, now re-named as North East Cane and 
Bamboo Development Council (NECBDC), is an ISO 9001:2008 certified organization and was 
established in the year 2000 as part of Cane and Bamboo Technological Upgradation and 
Networking Programme of United Nations Development Programme (UNDP), where the 
Department of Science & Technology (DST), Govt. of India and United Nations Industrial 
Development Organization (UNIDO) were the main stakeholders.  
 
1.8 North Eastern Handicrafts and Handlooms Development Corporation Limited 
(NEHHDC) 
 
1.8.1 NEHHDC was set up in 1977 at Shillong, Meghalaya, with the mandate of promotion and 
development of handicrafts and handlooms sectors in the NER of the country.  NEHHDC plays a 
key role in the development and promotion of the Handicrafts and Handlooms sectors of the 
North Eastern Region. NEHHDC has been providing Marketing support through procurement of 
Handicrafts and Handlooms products directly from the Artisans, Weavers, Self-Help Groups at 
clusters and selling the same through its Sales Emporia located at Shillong, Guwahati, Kolkata, 
New Delhi and Gujarat and through Sales Promotion Offices located at Chennai and Bangalore 
and also through the conduct of various exhibition and marketing events all over the country. 

5 
 
The Corporation achieved a gross turnover of Rs. 172.86 lakh during the period of 1st January, 
2020 to 31st December, 2020. 
 
1.9 North Eastern Regional Agricultural Marketing Corporation (NERAMAC)  
 
1.9.1  North Eastern Regional Agricultural Marketing Corporation (NERAMAC) Limited was 
incorporated in the year 1982 as a Government of India Enterprise under the administrative 
control of the Ministry of Development of North Eastern Region (DoNER).  
 
1.9.2  NERAMAC has been envisaged and functioning as a marketing organization dedicated to 
the agricultural sector of the North-eastern region and is lending a supporting hand to the 
farmers‟ beginning from the fields and up to the markets to the end consumers for their produces. 
NERAMAC procures produce from the farmers at remunerative prices and maintains the supply 
chain for proper distribution and placing of produces in the markets throughout the country.   
 
LIVELIHOOD & ENTREPRENEURSHIP 
 
1.10 North East Rural Livelihood Project (NERLP) 
 
1.10.1 North East Rural Livelihood Project (NERLP) was launched in 2012. It was implemented 
by the Ministry of Development of North Eastern Region (MDoNER) and aided by World Bank 
to improve rural livelihoods especially that of women, unemployed youth and the most 
disadvantaged people by building community-based organizations and developing a partnership 
with Government and other institutions for knowledge, skill, credit, insurance, extension and 
market. 
 
1.11 North Eastern Region Community Resource Management Project (NERCORMP) 
 
1.11.1 NEC supports Community Based Sustainable Livelihood Projects i.e., North Eastern 
Region Community Resource Management Project (NERCORMP), which was launched in May, 
1999. NERCORMP is running under North Eastern Region Community Resource Management 
Society (NERCRMS) which is a registered Society under North Eastern Council). It is operating 
in two states Arunachal Pradesh and Manipur and covers two districts in each of the states. In 
Arunachal Pradesh, the project covers Changlang, Tirap and Longding districts and in Manipur – 
Churachandpur and Chandel districts. The objective of NERCORMP is to improve the 
livelihoods of vulnerable groups in a sustainable manner through improved management of their 
resource base in a way that contributes to the preservation and restoration of the environment.  
 
1.12 North Eastern Development Finance Corporation Limited (NEDFi) 
 
1.12.1 The North Eastern Development Finance Corporation Limited (NEDFi) is a public 
limited company registered under the Companies Act, 1956, in 1995. It is notified as a Public 
Financial Institution under Section 4A of the said Act and was registered as an NBFC [Non-
Depository-Systemically Important (ND-SI)] in 2002 with RBI. The core business of the 
Corporation are lending activities undertaken through the project finance schemes & 
microfinance schemes and provides fee-based Advisory & Consultancy Services. NEDFi plays 

6 
 
an important role in facilitating the setting up of new industrial and service sector projects in the 
North Eastern States. Ministry of DoNER provides interest-free loans to NEDFi to catalyze 
industrial growth and nurture entrepreneurship in the North East Region.  
 
1.13 Science & Technology Intervention North Eastern Region (STINER)  
 
1.13.1 The new initiative for Science & Technology Intervention in North Eastern Region 
(STINER) was envisaged by M/o DoNER in close collaboration with the Office of Principal 
Scientific adviser (PSA) to Govt. of India with an aim to improve the livelihood of vulnerable 
groups especially that of women, unemployed youth and the poor farmers and artisans of the 
North Eastern Region of the country in a sustainable manner. The Ministry's initiative in the 
form of the STINER Project was officially transferred to North Eastern Council, Shillong along 
with a budget Provision of Rs. 40 crore in June, 2016 for sanctioning funds to implement the 
project by CSIR-NEIST, Jorhat. NEC released Rs. 27 crore as 1 st installment on 10 th August, 
2018 towards the STINER project. STINER main center, designated as STINER-TFC Hub 
(STINER Technology Facility Centre Hub) is established at CSIR-NEIST, Jorhat, Assam. In 
addition to the technology mall, the hub will also have a quality testing laboratory for food and 
other products to support the growing demand of food processing industries, food processing 
shelf help groups, NGOs working on food and allied sector to promote regional and export 
market of food in the region. Along with the STINER- TFC Hub, eight spokes will also be 
established in eight states of North East India. Already, Nagaland spoke has been established at 
School for Agricultural Science & Rural Development (SASRD), Nagaland University, 
Mezdiphema and activities have been initiated.  
 
1.14 Fight Against COVID-19 
 
1.14.1 2019-20 has been an unprecedented year due to COVID-19 Pandemic and NER is no  
exception to its effects. The Minsitry of DoNER made all efforts to fight the global pandemic. As 
a containment measure, all guidelines/advisories issued by the Ministry of Home Affairs/ 
Ministry of Health & Family Welfare/ Cabinet Secretariat/ Department of Personnel & Traini ng 
are followed strictly in MDoNER as well as in the Organizations under it. MDoNER had 
identified two quarantine facilities viz., CBTC Hostel Block, Burnihat, Guwahati, Assam and 
NEC House, New Delhi immediately on the on-set of the pandemic. One-day salary of the 
officers and staff of MDoNER and its Organizations has been deposited in PM CARES Fund. 
North Eastern Development Finance Corporation Ltd. (NEDFi), MDoNER has contributed its 
CSR funds of Rs.2.00 crore to PM CARES Fund. 
 
1.14.2 During to the on-set of COVID-19, the Ministry/NEC in the first week of April 2020, 
provided Rs.25.29 crores to the North Eastern States for gap funding to fight the COVID-19 
outbreak effectively. (Rs.4.99 crore to Assam, Rs.3.55 crore to Arunachal Pradesh, Rs.1.75 
crores to Sikkim and Rs.3.00 crore each to remaining NE States).  NEC also sanctioned a sum of 
Rs.24.96 crore toward livelihood generation for returned migrant workers of NER due to the 
pandemic. 
 
 

7

Special Infrastructure Development Scheme (NESIDS) has sanctioned five projects worth 
Rs.193.32 crore for strengthening health infrastructure to contain COVID-19 Pandemic. Against 
these projects, Rs.77.33 crore has been released by the Ministry to the State Governments.

1.14.4 NEC has provided Rs. 25.29 crore to the NE States as a gap funding to effectively fight 
the COVID-19 outbreak. The detail of funds released state-wise is in Table 1.3. NEC under its

1.14.5 The efforts of NE States on containing the COVID-19 pandemic is noteworthy. The 
number of positive COVID-19 patients exponentially increased after the return of migrant 
laborers to their native states. This was mitigated with the help of ramped up medical facilities 
and the provision of medical supplies using special flights and helicopters to the far-flung areas. 
This resulted in lower 'case positivity', high 'recovery rate' and lower 'case fatality in NE State s 
compared to the national average. 
 
***** 
 
  

9 
 
CHAPTER-II 
ASSESSMENT OF DEMANDS FOR GRANTS 
 
2.1 Budget at a Glance 
 
2.1.1 The Ministry of Development of North Eastern Region (DoNER) has been allocated a total 
net budget of Rs. 2658.00 crore for the financial year 2021-22, of which Rs. 2031.51 crore is 
under the Revenue Head and Rs. 626.49 crore is under the Capital Head. The total demand 
projected by the Ministry for 2021-22 was Rs. 4859.28 crore against which only Rs. 2658.00 
crore has been provided at BE stage. Due to this, the total outlay has been reduced to Rs. 2031.51 
crore in BE 2021-22 from Rs. 2330.14 crore in BE 2020-21. It remained the same in percentage 
terms of allocation under the Revenue Section. 
 
2.1.2 The allocation for the Scheme of NEC including NEC- Secretariat (Major Head – 2552 & 
2070) was Rs. 384.55 crore which was less than the projected demand and was therefore not 
sufficient. The allocation for North East Special Infrastructure Development Scheme (NESIDS) - 
Programme, NLCPR- State, NERSDS-EAP and Special Development Packages (BTC, KAATC 
and DHATC) is Rs. 630.00 crore, Rs. 404.50 crore, Rs. 321.65 crore and Rs. 70.75 crore, 
respectively, against the projected demand of Rs. 1300.00 crore, Rs. 750.00 crore, Rs. 398.36 
crore and Rs. 200.00 crore. This is likely to affect infrastructure development in the North 
Eastern Region. 
 
2.1.3 In the meeting held on 23 rd February, 2021, Secretary, M/o DoNER has stated before the 
Committee that the Ministry co-ordinates with other Ministries, reviews their expenditure on 
holding regular quarterly meetings with them. The review meeting is held not only to assess how 
much they have spent but also to see if there are some Ministries that need additional funds.  At 
the same time, if some Ministries have not been able to use the funds, the Ministry of DoNER 
recommends reallocation of resources within the overall pool and this year also they have done 
that for some Ministries.  The budgetary allocations to the Ministry of DoNER have been very 
meager. Budget Estimate for 2020-21 was Rs.3,048 crore which was reduced to 60 % level, i.e. 
Rs.1,868 crore only.  Keeping in view the quarterly and monthly expenditure limits imposed by 
the Department of Expenditure, the Ministry has utilized Rs.1,696 crore, that is, 91 % by now.  
In the last financial year 2019-20, Revised Estimates was used at 100 % level as part of the next 
five year's vision for the North-Eastern Region. 
 
2.1.4 On being asked if the Ministry is satisfied with the allocated funds, the Ministry replied 
that the total demand projected by the Ministry of DoNER under Demand No. 22 in General 
Budget for 2021-22 was Rs. 4859.28 crore against which only Rs. 2658.00 crore has been 
provided at BE stage. The total committed liability of the Ministry was Rs. 5292.39 crore as on  
31st March, 2020. The allocation may not be sufficient to meet the committed liabilities and 
requirements of the Ministry. 
 
2.1.5 The Committee expresses its concerns over the fact that there is a severe shortfall in 
the allocation in BE 2020-21 against the projected demand by the Ministry. This shortfall is 
particularly reflected in Schemes of NEC (MH-2552), NLCPR State (MH-3601),  NESIDS- 
Programme (MH-2552). The Committee fails to understand how the Ministry will b e able 

10 
 
to implement its approved infrastructure projects with this reduced allocation. The 
Committee, therefore, strongly recommends that the MDoNER must place their demand 
before the Ministry of Finance for increasing allocations at RE Stage so that the completion 
schedule of infrastructure projects may not get hampered due to shortage of funds.

2020-21 in actual terms as well as in percentage terms are given below:-

12 
 
2.2.4 As may be seen from the table above, the Ministry could not utilize the allocation to meet 
their financial targets under various schemes for which the Ministry has furnished the reasons as 
under:- 
 
(A)      The utilization was less in Schemes of NEC in 2020-21 due to non-receipt of Utilization 
Certificates for ongoing projects and exhaustion of funds for taking up of new 
projects. Accordingly, outlay for schemes of NEC under the Major Head Rs. 2552.00 crore was 
reduced from Rs. 869.90 crore to Rs. 512.94 crore (as on 31 st December, 2020) at the RE 2020-
21 stage.  The provision was for clearing committed liabilities of ongoing projects.  
 
(B)       The utilization was 100% for NLCPR-State and 100% for NLCPR- Central in 2019-20. It 
was 89.37% for NLCPR-State and 100% for NLCPR- Central in 2020-21. These schemes have 
been closed for taking up new projects and the provision was only for the release of funds for 
committed liabilities, which depend on receipt of Utilization Certificates from State 
Governments and implementing agencies, respectively.  
 
(C) There has been underutilization of the budget allocation in the North East Special 
Infrastructure Development Scheme (NESIDS) which was 96.58% in 2020-21 (as on 31 st 
December, 2020) as compared to 99.98% in 2019-20. However, now work orders are being 
received from the State Governments and full utilization of the Revised Estimate is expected. 
Similar is the reason for less utilization in the Hill Area Development Programme. 
 
(D)       The scheme of Advocacy & Publicity and Capacity Enhancement for Governanc e in 
NER was closed in the Ministry of DoNER in 2018-19 and merged with schemes of NEC.  The 
budget was transferred to NEC at the RE stage. 
 
(E)       North-East Rural Livelihood Project (NERLP), was a World Bank assisted Project which 
was concluded on 30 th September, 2019. It was extended upto 31 st December, 2019; only for 
closure-related activities pertaining to audit issues.  The Self Help Groups (SHGs) formed under 
NERLP were handed over to State Rural Livelihood Missions (SRLMs) under the DAY-NRLM 
Scheme of the Ministry of Rural Development. Hence, no funds have been allocated to NERLP. 
 
(F)       The utilization was 100% for North-Eastern Road Sector Development Scheme 
(NERSDS) - Programme (NERSDS) in 2019-20. It was 79.11% in 2020-21. Presently, NERSDS 
is 100% centrally funded and allocation approved by Cabinet for the period 2017-18 to 2019-20 
was Rs.1000 crore. An interim extension for the continuance of the scheme during 2020-21 for 
an outlay of Rs.340 crore has been granted by the competent authority in accordance with 
instructions of DoE in OM No.42 (02)/PF-II/2014 dated 10th January, 2020. 
 
(G)  Hill Area Development Programme (HADP) has been allocated 54.00 crore at BE stage, 
25.05 crore at RE stage. On being asked about the low utilization of the allocation (48.06% of 
RE), MDoNER replied that appropriate measures have been taken to increase expenditure and 
Rs. 23.88 crore (i.e. 95.32% of RE) have been spent as on 28th February, 2021. 
 
(H)      The packages of BTC and DHATC and SIDF also witnessed under-utilization in 2019-20 
and 2020-21. Reasons for under-utilization were late receipt of project proposals during the 
financial year and non-receipt of Utilization Certificates (UCs) from the State Government. 
 

13 
 
2.2.5 The Committee is disappointed to notice that the Ministry failed to utilize even the 
reduced funds which were allocated at RE stage for various plans schemes li ke NLCPR, 
NERSDS, KAATC, DHATC, etc. This under-utilization is unacceptable in these critical 
Schemes as it adversely affects the momentum of implementation of these programs. The  
Committee, therefore, strongly recommends that the Ministry of DoNER must impress 
upon all the concerned line Ministries/Departments/Agencies to take urgent steps for  
enhancing their capacity to utilize allocated funds fully. 
 
2.2.6 There has been under utilization of allocation of funds granted at BE stage under the 
Social and Infrastructure Development Fund (SIDF) on a consistent basis year after year i.e. 
29.3% in 2015-16, 28.1% in 2016-17, 42.6% in 2017-18, 69.72% in 2019-20 and 19.81% in 
2020-21 (upto 31st December, 2020).  
 
2.2.7 In this regard, the Committee had expressed its anguish over the dismal utilization 
under this Fund in its 225 th Report on Demand for Grants (2020-21) of the Ministry of 
DoNER. It is disappointing to note that the allocation under SIDF has been reduced at the 
RE stage.  
 
2.2.8 The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Ministry must furnish a detailed 
project-wise utilization schedule of funds under SIDF and the steps taken to improve the 
utilization. 
 
2.2.9 The Committee also notes the utilization of 50.90% under North East Road Sector 
Development Scheme- Programme in 2020-21. The Committee further recomm ends that 
the Ministry should hold periodic meetings with the concerned States to guide them and  to 
resolve the issues of late receipt of project proposals and non-receipt of Utilization  
Certificates in time from the State Governments.  
 
2.2.10  During the Committee's meeting held on 23 rd February 2021, Secretary, M/o DoNER 
stated that reasons for under-utilization are late receipt of project proposals and Utilization 
Certificates from the State Governments.  With respect to 10% Gross Budget Support (GBS) by 
Central Ministries and Departments, Budget Estimate for the year 2021-21 was Rs.60,112 crore 
which has been reduced at RE stage to Rs.51,270 crore, against which, as on 31 st December, 
2020, an amount of Rs.32,719 crore has been spent which comes to 63.8 % of the RE. The 
unutilized portion of 10 % GBS gets transferred to a non-lapsable Central pool of resources 
which is maintained on a proforma basis by the Department of Expenditure.  The Ministry has 
taken up the matter with the Ministry of Finance during October, 2019 for leveraging of this pool 
funds for undertaking certain important projects for NER. In addition to this, MoDoNER 
regularly conducts review meetings with all the Ministries with a view to ensure maximum 
utilization of 10% GBS resources.  
 
2.2.11 On being asked about the reasons for consistent low utilization of 10% Gross Budgetary 
Support (GBS), the Ministry has replied that the reason for not utilizing 100% of RE during 
2014-2020 varies from Ministry to Ministry and from year to year. One of the major reasons for 
poor performance of beneficiary-oriented Schemes of various Ministries is the lack of target 
population in adequate numbers due to which full absorption of ten percent of the Scheme 

14 
 
budget in NER is not possible. The other major reasons include lack of sufficient eligible 
proposals due to less scope for complete utilization in a specific sector; poor capacity in States 
for the formulation of projects as per schematic guidelines; lack of knowledge of Schemes and 
procedural guidelines of line Ministries; poor progress in providing UCs etc hampers timely 
releases by Union Ministries. Additionally, lack of suitable implementing agencies and delay due 
to the short working season, difficult terrain, Bandhs, Law & order problems leads to delay in 
implementation of projects. To counter these bottlenecks, regular meetings have been held and 
are being held with Central Ministries/Departments and the NE States to review the status of 
utilization of budgetary resources under 10% GBS of non-exempt Ministries, as well as Minist ry 
of DoNER and requisite measures are being taken to ensure maximum absorption of funds by the 
NE States.  Secretaries of Central Ministries/Departments and Chief Secretaries of NE State, 
through various communications from the Ministry of DoNER have been asked to ensure full 
utilization of earmarked resources, if necessary, by formulating new NE-specific 
schemes/projects or modifying the existing schemes as per local need and situation.  Further, NE 
State are being told to expedite implementation of various projects/schemes already sanctioned, 
reduce unspent balances and furnish UCs of funds already released.  NEC has also been asked to 
coordinate with Central Ministries and NE States for ensuring full utilization of 10% GBS. 
 
2.2.12  In February, 2021, Secretary, MDoNER has taken up the matter of low expenditure 
under 10% GBS with his counterparts in the concerned Ministries/Departments. He has 
requested them to issue appropriate instructions to concerned officers of their Ministry to take 
requisite steps to increase the pace of implementation of various schemes in NER or design 
suitable Schemes to fully absorb the budget allocation in the current year.

Committee that there is a regular and continuous interaction with respective 
Ministries/Departments for effective coordination among them. The Ministry of DoNER also 
convenes sectoral review meetings with key Ministries such as Railways, Road Transport &

15 
 
effective implementation of schemes and time-bound completion of major infrastructure projects 
in the North Eastern Region. The Ministry of DoNER is represented in Inter-Ministerial Groups 
of major Ministries such as MoRTH, Railways, MSME, Home, etc. and is also consulted at 
policy formation and project implementation stage through Expenditure Finance Committee 
(EFC)/ State Financial Corporation (SFC) and Cabinet notes.

 2.2.16 The Committee notes the step taken by the Ministry in recommending reallocation 
of resources within the overall pool to the other Ministries and observes that in the NER, 
the developmental process has become very compartmentalized. Moreover, the 
departments of the Ministries are working in silos which have led to major funds 
remaining unutilized. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Ministry must 
strengthen the mechanism for better coordination among the other Ministries/Departments 
for improved utilization of the allocated funds. The Committee further recom mends that 
the Ministry should strengthen the project management system and all pending proje cts 
should be completed by the Ministry in a Special Mission mode drive. 
 
2.2.17  The Committee further recommends that the concerned Ministries/Departments  
and Agencies working under the control of the Ministry of DoNER should be directed to 
adhere strictly to the time line and evenly control the expenditure in all the quarters of the 
Financial Year. They should also be asked to desist from incurring hasty expenditure at the 
end of the Financial Year.

2.7.3     As may be seen from the table above, the Committee observes that there is a shortfall in 
the amount allocated in BE 2021-22 in the Capital Section due to a reduction in the budget 
outlay of the Ministry. The allocation for the North-Eastern Council (NEC)- Capital (Major Head 
Rs.4,552 crore) and North East Road Sector Development Scheme (NERSDS) – Programme 
component is Rs. 42.00 crore and Rs. 374.04 crore against the projected demand of Rs. 57.00 
crore and Rs. 690.00 crore respectively. The allocation under Loan to NEDFi is Rs. 30.00 crore 
as against the proposed demand of Rs. 60.00 crore, due to shortfall in projected loan to NEDFi, 
the financial and promotional activities, etc. will get adversely affected in NER. The reduction in 
capital outlay is likely to affect infrastructure development in North Eastern Region. It may  also 
be noted that although 23.57% of funds are provided under Capital Section, almost 97% of BE 
2021-22 of the Ministry are meant for capital expenditure though the large portion is classified 
under Revenue Section. 
 
2.7.4 The Committee is of the view that this shortfall may adversely affect the 
implementation of several projects and institutions. There has been marginal under -
utilization of allocated funds under Heads of North Eastern Council (NEC) and North-East 
Road Sector Development Scheme (NERSDS) for the year 2020-21. Hence, the Committee  
recommends that MDoNER should strive to achieve higher utilization of allocated funds. 
At the same time, it should also persuade the Ministry of Finance to allocate a larger  
amount of funds for these Schemes.

CHAPTER-III 
 
NORTH EASTERN COUNCIL (NEC) AND RELATED SCHEMES 
 
3.1.1 The North Eastern Council which was set up as a Regional Advisory Body under the 
North Eastern Council Act, 1971 (Act No. 84 of 1971), was transformed into a Regional 
Planning Body by the Amendment of 2002.  The relevant provision of the Amended NEC Act is 
quoted below: - 
 
Section 4 of the original Act: 
 
“(a) for sub-section (1) and (2), the following sub-sections shall be substituted, namely:- 
1. The Council shall function as a regional planning body for the North eastern area. 
2. While formulating the regional plans for the Northeastern area, the Council shall give 
priority to schemes and projects, which will benefit two or more States: 
Provided that in case of Sikkim, the Council shall formulate specific projects and schemes for 
that State including the review of the implementation of such project and schemes". 
 
3.1.2 The Committee was informed that the above provision of the amended NEC Act 
mandated the North Eastern Council (NEC) to function as a "regional planning body for the 
North Eastern Area" with the tasks of formulating "regional plans for the North Eastern Area" 
that benefit two or more States. In view of the landmass and population of the majority of the 
States in the NE Region, it is felt that it will be beneficial for such States if regional plans are 
formulated in a coordinated manner for the cohesive and integrated economic development of 
the entire region. 
 
3.2 Schemes of NEC 
 
3.2.1 The details of actual expenditure incurred by NEC during 2019-20 and allocations made 
in 2020-21 and 2021-22 is as under:- 
Table 3.1 
   (Rs. in crore) 
 Actual 
2019-20

2021-22 
Revenue 346.84 526.18 360.59 342.55 
Capital 25.00 80.00 13.79 42.00 
Total  371.84 606.18 374.38 384.55 
 
3.2.2 The Committee was informed that the "Schemes of the NEC" has now become a Central 
Sector Scheme with 100% funding by the Government of India. However, the on-going projects 
under the Scheme of NEC- Special Development Projects with existing funding pattern (90:10) 
will continue till March 2020 through the State treasury.  
 
 
 

23 
 
3.2.3   The “NEC General Guidelines” were first prepared in 2010 and laid down the importance 
of preparation of perspective plans with regional approach; procedures to be followed for 
obtaining priority list from the NE States; selection, appraisal, sanction, monitoring and 
reporting, evaluation of projects; composition and functions of various committees and other 
details for NEC to follow.  
  
3.2.4 The Committee was further informed that the Union Cabinet in its meeting held in 
January 2020 approved earmarking of 30 percent of North Eastern Council's (NEC's) allocation 
for new projects under the existing "Schemes of North Eastern Council" for focused 
development of deprived areas; deprived/neglected sections of society and emerging priority 
sectors in the Northeastern States. The balance allocation shall be bifurcated in the existing two 
components viz. State component-60 percent and Central component-40 percent.  The Union 
Cabinet also approved allowing projects under State component, up to 25 percent of each State's 
normative allocation, for sectors not covered in the mandate of NEC, but which are considered 
important as per locally felt needs and as per the recommendations of the State Governments. 
Projects to be undertaken under the 25% flexibility clause would generally be of a bigger size to 
have optimum output and outcomes as well as for the avoidance of a thin spread of resources. 
Accordingly, General guidelines for NEC were revised in April 2020 to reflect the above Cabinet 
decisions.  
 
3.2.5 The Ministry of DoNER through its Annual Report, (2020-21) informed the Committee 
that NEC is in the process of compiling the list of such deprived/neglected sections/areas of 
society. Indicative List of areas/sectors for focused development of deprived areas; 
deprived/neglected sections of society and emerging priority sectors in the Northeastern States 
are as below:- 
  
a. Programmes for afforestation of denuded hills in NER. The plantation will involve tall-
growing trees other than bamboo.  
b. Programmes/projects for the development of organic farming in NER.  
c. Commissioning of studies to look into problems of ethnic groups in NER like Bru, 
Chakmas, etc. and possible solutions to the identified problems.  
d. Documentation of disputes between different States of North East for further action by 
MHA.  
e. Study of various languages/scripts of the NE States and measures to revive the lost 
languages/scripts.  
f. Such other projects of importance for focused development of deprived areas, vulnerable 
and neglected groups/sections of society and other emerging priority sectors.  
 
3.2.6    Furthermore, as per the guidelines of 2018, certain sectors were omitted from the purview 
of NEC, but since then, states were projecting the need for funds in those sectors were available. 
Accordingly in the guidelines of 2020, a flexibility of using 25% of the state component of 
NEC's allocation, for sectors not covered under its mandate has been given so that projects which 
are felt necessary in the sectors out of mandated one can also be considered for sanction. 
 
 

24 
 
3.2.7    Hence, as per NEC's guidelines 2020, 30% of NEC's allocation for the FY 2020-21 is 
Rs.212 crore, out of which projects worth Rs.177 crore have already been sanctioned and 
balance projects are in the process of sanctioning. The details of projects sanctioned are given 
below:

3.2.8 The Ministry further informed in its reply to the questionnaire that the allocation of Rs. 
606.18 crore (Capital & Revenue) in BE 2020-21, under the Schemes of the North East Council 
Head, has been reduced to Rs. 384.55 crore in BE 2021-22. The reduction at RE 2020-21 stage 
i.e. Rs. 374.38 crore was done by the Ministry of Finance, Department of Expenditure due to 
restrictions of expenditure on account of Covid-19 Pandemic. Expenditure incurred up to 31 st 
December, 2020 was Rs.331.19 crore.  The projected demand in FY 2021-22 is Rs.57.00 crore 
under Capital Head against which the allocation has been made Rs.42.00 crore. The decrease in 
allocation due to reduction in the Budget Outlay in 2021-22 of the Ministry would be sufficient 
to carry out proposed activities under the Head.

25 
 
3.2.9 The Committee sought to know schemes undertaken by the North East Council in 2020-
21 to ensure integrated socio-economic development of states of Northeast and significant 
achievements made so far. The Ministry in its reply informed the committee that since its 
inception, the NEC has strived to serve the needs of NE States through its focus on Region's 
special circumstances and needs. Despite many constraints, it has been assisting in the planning 
and development efforts of the States of the Region and making continuous efforts towards faster 
socio-economic development of NER. For this purpose, a number of schemes, both Central and 
State, have been implemented through NEC. Blockades in implementation of the projects are 
related to various reasons in NE Regions viz. law and order, insurgency problems and the issue 
of land acquisition and land availability, limited working seasons, natural calamities, difficult 
geographical terrain, delay in the release of funds from the financial Departments of the 
concerned States to the different implementation agencies, etc.   
 
3.2.10 As per the directions of the Department of Expenditure, the NEC has conducted an 
independent third-party evaluation of NEC Schemes and NERSDS.  The third-party evaluation 
for the "Schemes of NEC" had been entrusted to the Centre for Good Governance (CGG), 
Hyderabad. The evaluator has found the Scheme very effective for the speedy and holistic 
development of NER as the majority of the ongoing projects are delivering or will deliver the 
intended and planned results/objectives. 
 
3.2.11 The Committee sought to know as to how far the schemes of North East Council, since 
their inception, have been successful in terms of accelerating the balanced and integrated 
economic development of the region. The Ministry in its reply to the Committee informed that, 
in the last forty-eight years, NEC has been instrumental in setting up iconic institutes in the 
region such as North East Police Academy, Shillong, North Eastern Regional Institute of Science 
& Technology, Itanagar, Regional Institute of Paramedical & Nursing Sciences, Aizawl, 
Regional Institute of Medical Sciences, Imphal, North Eastern Regional Institute of Water and 
Land Management in Tezpur, Regional Institute of Pharmaceutical Science & Technology 
(RIPSAT), Agartala and others. A total of 10,911 km. of roads have been constructed with the 
NEC funding and handed over to the States for maintenance. NEC has supported the installation 
of 694.5 MW of power plants and the construction of 9202 circuit Km of transmission & 
distribution lines. Besides, NEC has also funded 11 Inter-State Bus terminus (ISBT) projects, 3 
Inter-State Truck Terminus, improvement of the infrastructure in 5 major airports and other 
development works in sectors like education, healthcare, agriculture, horticulture, tourism, 
industries, etc. NEC has also been implementing various projects in different sectors such as 
Health, Irrigation & Flood Control, Industries, Tourism, Human Resource Development, Sports, 
Science & Technology, Agriculture & Horticulture, Fisheries and Art & Culture, etc.  
  
3.2.12 The Committee was apprised that the implementation of projects in the NE states faces 
unique challenges, especially short working seasons and lack of big infrastructure contracting 
firms. However, in recent years the situation and implementing capacities have considerably 
improved in project implementations by the States and Agencies.  
 
3.2.13 The Ministry informed that the main bottlenecks faced by the NEC were late receipt of 
Utilization Certificates (UCs); delay in awarding the work; delay in the release of funds by State 
Governments to implementing agencies, the slow pace of implementation of projects due to 

26 
 
difficult terrain and reduced working season. These have been addressed by frequent review 
meetings with the senior officers of the State Governments, and close coordination and 
communication between MDoNER and NE State Governments for project implementation. 
Ministry of DoNER has appointed State-wise Chief Nodal Officers/Nodal Officers for effective 
implementation/monitoring of projects funded under various schemes of this Ministry.  In order 
to avoid delay in the release of funds by the State Government to Implementing Agencies, all the 
funds are released by the Ministry to the State Government through Public Finance Management 
System (PFMS) portal. Further, the State Government has also been directed to implement EAT 
module of PFMS, and release funds to implementing agencies through this system only. Other 
steps taken to strengthen the monitoring mechanism include an online portal for specific 
schemes/projects; payment through PFMS; Geo-tagging of project sites; monitoring of physical 
progress through geotagged photographs; 'just in time' release of funds (linked to physical and 
financial progress) and inspection of major projects by Technical Wing of the Ministry, on a 
sample basis. 
 
3.2.14 The Committee notes that the allocation has been reduced to 384.55 crore in BE 
2021-22 from 606.18 crore in BE 2020-21. The Committee further notes that against the 
projected demand of Rs.57.00 crore under Capital Head in FY 2021-22 only Rs. 42 crore 
has been allocated for NEC.   The Committee is of the view that the reduced allocation 
might hamper the progress of various ongoing and proposed infrastructure projects. The  
Committee, therefore, recommends to seek additional funds particularly, under the Capital 
Head from the Ministry of Finance at RE stage.  
 
3.2.15 The Committee further recommends that the Ministry of DoNER should implement 
the suggestions of the Centre for Good Governance (CGG), Hyderabad for the 
development of sector-specific and project-specific DPR template, system-driv en DPR 
appraisal and approval system, quarterly consultation with State Governments,  
sustainability of projects, etc. The Committee also notes the persistent problem of late  
receipt of Utilization Certificates (UCs) from State Governments and recommen ds that the 
Ministry may take up this matter with concerned State Governments on a continuous basis 
and closely monitor the progress of the project to ensure submission of Utilization 
Certificates from the State Governments within a fixed time frame for the ongoing an d 
future projects. 
 
3.2.16  During the Committee's meeting held on 23 rd February, 2021, Secretary, NEC  stated that 
NEC takes up a number of works including roads, horticulture, agriculture, higher education, 
health, treasury, etc. It also takes care of deprived sections and deprived areas.  It has been 
working closely with eight North Eastern States for the welfare of deprived communities like 
Chakmas, Brus , Koch, etc. Organic Farming has become the major focus of NEC's strategy 
along with afforestation. Tourism has been a major driver of economic development and 
employment opportunities in NER along with the improvement of IT infrastructure. NEC has 
been closely evaluating with respect to these aspects for better implementation of the related 
schemes. 
 
 

27 
 
3.2.17 On being asked about the steps being taken to sort out tribal ethnic problems and steps for 
the benefits of the Chakma community residing outside Chakma Autonomous Council, the 
Ministry replied that various initiatives have been taken for addressing the development gaps in 
respect of Chakmas. Community Development projects amounting to Rs 5.41 crore have been 
sanctioned to Mamim District, Mizoram for the benefit of Brus and Chakmas in that district. Rs 
6.05 Crore development projects have been appropriated for Chakmas through the Chakma 
Autonomous District Council, Mizoram. 28 value chain and food processing units costing Rs 
9.88 Crore have been sanctioned for the benefit of Chakmas and other minor tribes of Tirap & 
Changlang Districts of Arunachal Pradesh.  
 
3.2.18 The Committee notes that important measures have been taken by the Governmen t 
towards upliftment of the tribal ethnic population of the Region. The Committee also 
observes that still various socio-economic hardships and physiological issues are being 
faced by them particularly, by the Idu Mishmi Tribe of Arunachal Pradesh which h as led 
to frequent suicides in that Tribe. The Committee notes that the North Eastern Region is 
endowed with rich cultural heritage. Some villages though very small in size, possess 
distinct culture like the unique Kongthong village which is also called a whistling village 
with a population of 650 only. The Committee is of the considered view that protection of 
these tribes is important as they represent the cultural heritage and diversity of the North-
Eastern Region. Therefore, the Committee, recommends, that a micro-level study shou ld 
be conducted to understand the problems and issues being faced by the tribals so th at 
effective measures might be initiated to address them. Further, an effective monitor ing 
mechanism also needs to be developed for the implementation of the welfare measu res 
along with special attention to cater to the specific needs of every tribe instead of taking an 
umbrella approach for the NER.

3.3.2 The Ministry informed that the Special Development Projects under "Schemes of the 
NEC" has now become a Central Sector Scheme with 100% funding by the Government of 
India. However, the on-going projects under the Scheme of NEC- Special Development Projects 
with existing funding pattern (90:10) will continue till March 2020 through the State treasury. As 
a step towards ensuring equitable allocation of resources and benefits amongst all NE States, the 
Government has approved the normative allocation of 60 % of the NEC budget among the 8 NE 
states as per a formula based on various development indices. This normative allocation is 

28 
 
included in the guidelines and is being followed while releasing funds to the eight states. 60% 
NEC budget will be utilized for funding other projects where the proposing state has a major 
stake but which also benefits other states, except in the case of Sikkim where projects benefitting 
only that state may also be taken up. The main goals and objectives of these schemes are the 
holistic and inclusive growth of the North Eastern Region, the NEC has prepared a well-
researched and pragmatic "North Eastern Region: Vision 2020" document with an elaborated 
consultative approach across the region.  This sub-scheme is now being merged in the Scheme of 
NEC, as the Scheme of NEC has become a Central Sector Scheme with 100% funding by the 
Government of India. 
 
3.3.3 The total allocation of Rs. 263.72 crore at BE stage in 2020-21, has been reduced to Rs. 
200.00 crore in BE 2021-22. The Committee was informed that the allocation under this Head 
are for committed liabilities of on-going projects and no new project is proposed to be taken up 
under this Head. The projected amount under this Head is Rs. 203.72 crore. The earmarked 
amount to the tune of Rs. 200.00 crore is expected to be sufficient for the intended purpose. 
 
3.3.4 The Committee notes that the allocation under this Head are for committed 
liabilities of on-going projects and no new project is proposed to be taken up unde r this 
Head. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Ministry must expedite its effor ts to 
clear liabilities of the on-going projects within this prescribed time for the over-all 
development of the North-Eastern Region. 
 
Schemes of NEC

and the projects undertaken from it. The Ministry informed that during the period 2020-21, 137 
projects have been sanctioned under “Schemes of NEC”, at an estimated approved cost of Rs. 
756.12 crore. During 2020-21, 04 projects have been sanctioned under NERSDS, at an estimated 
approved cost of Rs. 223.40 crore. As on 31 st January, 2021, 111 projects costing Rs. 841.35 
crore were completed.

3.4 Central Pool of Resources for North East and Sikkim 
 
3.4.1 The Non - Lapsable Central Pool of Resources (NLCPR) Scheme of the Ministry aims to 
fill up gaps in the infrastructure sector of the North Eastern Region through financial assistance 
to the projects prioritized by the North East State Governments. The Ministry gets annual 
budgetary allocation for funding projects under NLCPR Scheme. Funding of projects under the 
NLCPR scheme is on a 90:10 sharing pattern between Central and State Governments. The 
scheme has been re-structured as North East Infrastructure Development Scheme (NESIDS) 
w.e.f. 15 th December, 2017 with 100% central funding for projects of specified sectors of 
physical and social infrastructure. 
 
3.4.2 The following table shows allocation under Revenue and Capital Heads in BE 2021-22 
for Central Pool of Resources for North East and Sikkim  against actual expenditure during 2019-
20 and BE & RE 2020-21:-

3.5 Non-Lapsable Central Pool of Resources (NLCPR-State) 
 
3.5.1 The Ministry informed in its reply to the questionnaire about the reasons for the increase 
of allocation in BE 2020-21, that out of the allocation of Rs.580.95 crore, the allocation of Non-
Lapsable Central Pool of Resources-State (NLCPR-State) is Rs.404.50 crore. The increased 
allocation is for meeting the committed liabilities against the ongoing projects under NLCPR-
State Scheme, which is around Rs.1592.11 crore. During the period from April to September, 
2020, an expenditure of Rs.73.60 crore was incurred under the NLCPR-State scheme.  
 
3.5.2 The Committee was further informed by the Ministry in its reply to the questionnaire that 
the increase of allocation of Rs.176.45 crore under the NLCPR-Central Scheme has been 
proposed for meeting the committed liabilities under the scheme which is Rs.239.00 crore as on 
31st December, 2020.   
 
3.5.3 The Ministry stated that to speed up the implementation of the projects and in pursuance 
of the Government initiative of Maximum Governance with Minimum Government, NLCPR 
Guidelines were last revised in 2016 to make the process of retention and sanction of projects 
more transparent and participative. The new provisions in the guidelines empowered State 
Governments, put a cap on retention of projects, discouraged parking of funds and encouraged 
timely completion of projects. The salient features of Revised Guidelines, 2016 are as under:- 
 
(i) Constitution of State Level Empowered Committee (SLEC) in each State to 
prioritize and recommend projects for retention/sanction. 
(ii) Delegation of power of vetting the DPRs, techno- economically, to State Level 
Empowered Committee (SLEC). 
(iii) Value of Projects to be retained not to exceed three times normative allocation minus 
value of the retained project. 
(iv) Number of installments for the release of funds for sanctioned projects reduced to 
two. Now, the funds to be released in two installments of 40% and 60%. 
(v) To ensure the just-on-time release of funds, initially, a token amount of Rs.10 lakh to 
be released and the balance amount of the first installment to be released after receipt 
of the letter of award of the contract. 
 
3.5.4 When the Committee sought to know about the criteria for deciding the quantum of 
grants given to the States from the Central Pool of Resources and the steps that have been taken 
to assist State Governments of NER for preparing sound and feasible Detailed Project Reports 
(DPRs), the Ministry informed the Committee that funds are distributed amongst States on the 
basis of weightage attached to the parameters like Area, Population, Human Development Index, 
Road density, Percentage of census, etc. mentioned below: 
Table 3.7 
Sl. No Characteristics Weights attached 
1 Area 25%  
2 Population (census 2011) 25% 
3 Human Development Index  15% (in inverse proportion) 
4 Road density per 1000 sq. Km 7%  (in inverse proportion) 
5 Percentage of census villages Electrified  6%  (in inverse proportion) 
6 Number of Hospital beds (Allopathy) per 1000 
persons  
6%  (in inverse proportion) 

31 
 
7 Percentage of households having safe drinking water  6%  (in inverse proportion) 
8 Completion rate of NLCPR projects  10% 
 
  
 3.5.5 The Ministry further stated that since the NLCPR Scheme has been restructured into a 
new scheme NESIDS w.e.f. 15 th December, 2017, no new projects are being sanctioned under 
the NLCPR-State scheme. Under the new scheme of NESIDS, the State Govt. has been advised 
to engage the Institutions of repute like IITs/NITs, etc. for preparation of the DPRs.  
 
3.5.6 The Committee notes that as Non-Lapsable Central Pool of Resources (NLCPR) 
Scheme has been restructured into a new North East Special Infrastructure Develop ment 
Scheme (NESIDS) w.e.f. 15 th December, 2017, no new projects are being sanctioned under 
NLCPR-State Scheme. The Committee recommends that the ongoing project unde r the 
NLCPR-State Scheme should be completed within the stipulated time period without cost 
overrun. Further, Committee also desires to be informed about the progress status of the 
project under NCLPR-State Scheme that has been restructured w.e.f. 15th December, 2017. 
 
3.6 North East Special Infrastructure Development Scheme (NESIDS) 
 
3.6.1 The Cabinet has approved a new scheme of North East Special Infrastructure 
Development Scheme (NESIDS) on 15 th December, 2017 restructuring the existing NLCPR 
scheme of Ministry of DoNER.  NESIDS has been approved for a period of three years i.e. from 
2017-18 to 2019-20 at a total cost of Rs.1,600.00 crore. 
  
3.6.2 The broad objective of NESIDS is to ensure focused development of North East Region 
by providing financial assistance for the projects of:  
 
a. Physical infrastructure sectors relating to water supply, power, connectivity to enhance 
tourism;   
b. Social sector of education and health for creation of infrastructure in the areas of 
primary and secondary sectors. 
 
3.6.3 The Projects taken up under NESIDS will be 100% funded by Government of India. 
While identifying the projects, emphasis will be given to the backward areas by earmarking 25% 
share for them.  The NESIDS will be over and above the existing schemes of Government of 
India and State Governments of the NE Region. Under the scheme, only those projects would be 
taken up which are not covered under any scheme of Government of India or the State 
Government. The NLCPR savings of Rs. 402.47 crore has been carried forward to NESIDS. 
Since approval of NESIDS on 15 th December, 2017, projects worth Rs.1904.94 crore have 
already been selected for funding out of which 57 projects worth Rs. 1799.28 crore have been 
sanctioned so far.

3.7.2 A provision of Rs. 55 lakhs has been earmarked for the year 2021-22 for meeting the

assisted road projects in the NER. Responding to the Committee's query whether the earmarked

sanctioned staff of 9 [Project Coordinator-1, Consultants-4, Project Asistants-4,] Ministry of 
DoNER has been working with only three Project Assistants in the Project Management Unit 
(PMU). Hence, a provision of Rs. 55 lakhs would be sufficient for the intended purpose.

in NER was prepared by NEC during Financial Year 2003-04. The basis for development of 
roads in the region was this perspective plan. The plan was also incorporated in NER Vision 
2020. The process of monitoring is same as for NERSDS projects.

3.9 North East Road Sector Development Scheme  
 
3.9.1 The projects under NERSDS are conceptualized based on the approved guideline of 
NERSDS for selection of projects. The guidelines are as under :- 
(i) Inter-state roads previously built by the North Eastern Council (NEC) and other 
agencies, are of vital connectivity for one State but, of little importance for other State 
and hence remained neglected.  
(ii) Roads in socio-politically neglected pockets of NER; 
(iii) Roads required for security or strategic viewpoints, not covered in any other 
programmes; and  
(iv) Roads necessary from the view point of market access for agriculture produce and 
roads of economic importance on gap filling approach.  
 
3.9.2 The Ministry informed that 26 projects were identified by Expenditure Finance 
Committee (EFC), out of which 21 projects have already been sanctioned. Entire capital outl ay 
of Rs 1000.00 crore for the NERSDS, earmarked for the period from 2017 to March 2020 has 
been allocated for these projects. An interim extension for continuance of the Scheme during 
2020-21 for an outlay of Rs.340 crore has been granted by the competent authority in 
accordance with instructions of DoE in OM No.42(02)/PF-II/2014 dated 10.01.2020.

3.9.4 The Committee observes that out of the 36 projects proposed, only 8 have been 
sanctioned as indicated in the table above. Moreover, vital and important infrastructure 
projects like hospitals to fight Covid-19 Pandemic, schools are still in the process of being 
sanctioned. The Committee strongly recommends that all viable projects that are prepared 

39 
 
as per the fixed guidelines should be sanctioned at the earliest and implemented without 
time and cost overrun. 
 3.9.5 On being asked about steps taken to resolve the issue of blockade on Manipur Highway, 
The Ministry replied that historically, Kohima-Imphal Section of NH-02 (old NH-39) was 
blocked by agitators to put pressure on the State Govt. In order to overcome the problem, 
Ministry of Road Transport and Highways (MoRTH) through NHIDCL have taken up the 
improvement of the existing NH-37 (Old NH-53) from Imphal to Jiribam (on Assam/Manipur 
Border) and the NH-129 A from Maram to Dimapur.  In addition to these two alternatives, a new 
NH has been declared by MoRTH on 22 nd February, 2021 from Tamenglong in Manipur to near 
Mahur on East West Corridor in Assam.  Out of the total length of about 156 Km of this new 
NH, sanction for about 50 KM is under process during current financial year 2020-21 and the 
balance stretch will be taken up in the next Financial Year 2021-22.  Therefore, traffic 
commuting between Assam and Manipur need not cross Mao gate.  
3.9.6 The Committee sought to know the sources and pattern of funding under this scheme to 
which the Ministry replied that there is 100% funding by Government of India and the 
implementing agencies are States‟ PWD and CPWD as the case may be. The role of the Ministry 
of DoNER is to appraise and approve projects costing more than Rs.15 crore.  Projects costing 
upto Rs.15 crore are appraised and approved by Secretary, NEC and the monitoring mechanisms 
for NERSDS project is as follows: 
(i) Monitoring Mechanism at the State Govt. level; 
(ii) Periodical inspection by NEC Engineers for all the projects; 
(iii) Third Party Quality Control Assurances Mechanism for which provision of 1% 
Quality Control has been kept in the DPR. 
 
3.9.7 The Ministry further informed that it has been decided that in future, no project will be 
sanctioned unless the land is available free from all encumbrances; all forest and other regulatory 
clearances are available.  The use of space technology through NESAC by satellite mapping of 
proposed and ongoing projects and geo-tagging would be adopted intensively to overcome the 
geographical and physical challenges.  
3.9.8 The Committee appreciates the decision of the Ministry not to sanction any project  
unless the land is available free from all encumbrances and clearances from conc erned 
authorities are obtained and observes that this step will remove the obstacles and lead to 
timely completion of the proposed project at the sanctioned amount without cost escalation. 
This will also ensure that the strategically and economically important projects are not 
stalled due to legal wrangles.

3.11.3 The Committee observes that under the North East Road Sector Development 
Scheme (NERSDS), there are two components viz. Programme Component and Extern ally 
Aided Projects (EAP) . The Committee is of the considered view that North East Road 
Projects are strategically significant because most of the roads in NER are linked with the  
international borders which are used in the movement of security personnel and other 
essential commodities at the time of need, therefore, keeping these roads in serviceable 
condition is very crucial. These are also extremely important for the socio economic 
development of the Region. The Committee is displeased to observe the pace at which 
projects are being implemented. Various projects in NER are at 0% to 30% physical 
progress level in spite of funds being already released. The Committee therefore, stron gly
 
recommends for regular review and close monitoring for expeditious construction of these 
roads.

CHAPTER-IV 
SPECIAL DEVELOPMENT PACKAGES 
4.1  Bodoland Territorial Council (BTC) 
 
4.1.1 As per the Memorandum of Settlement (MoS), the Government of India agreed to 
provide a Special Economic Package over and above Plan fund to Bodoland Territorial Council 
(BTC) through the Government of Assam to undertake special projects for socio-economic and 
infrastructure development that are proposed by the Council.  The total value of the Special 
Economic Package is Rs.750.00 crore.  65 projects worth Rs. 749.64 crore have been sanctioned 
so far, out of which, 51 projects worth Rs. 570.19 crore have already been completed. The 
remaining 14 projects are at various stages of implementation. 
 
4.1.2 The Ministry has stated that against the commitment of Rs. 750.00 crore under the 
Special Economic Package, an amount of Rs. 711.19 crore has been released so far and 
utilization certificate of Rs. 677.23 crore have been received from the State Government. 
 
4.1.3 The following table reflects the fund allocation in BE 2021-22 to BTC under Special

4.1.4 Explaining the reason for enhancement of allocation from Rs. 10 crore in BE 2020-21 to 
Rs. 11 crore in BE 2021-22, the Ministry stated that the committed liabilities against the 14 
ongoing projects are around Rs.34.70 crore, which is based on the approved cost of ongoing 
projects. The actual liabilities may be less than Rs.34.70 crore. The release to the State 
Government in respect of the project is made based on the value of work order. 
 
4.1.5 On being asked about the problems that are being faced by the Council in undertaking 
developmental activities in the BTC area and what steps are being taken to combat the same, the 
Committee was apprised that there were some cases of delay in transfer of funds from the State 
Government and in order to avoid delay in release of funds by State Government to 
Implementing Agencies, all the funds are released by the Ministry to the State Governments 
through Public Finance Management System (PFMS) portal. Further, the State Government has 
also been requested to implement EAT module of PFMS. 
 

44 
 
4.1.6 Responding to the Committee's query about the further steps that can be taken for making 
Bodoland Territorial Council a harbinger of socio-economic development, the Ministry stated 
that as per clause 10.1 of the Memorandum of Settlement (MoS) signed on 27 th January, 2020, 
the Government of Assam may earmark a sum of Rs. 250 crore per annum for a period of three 
years for development of area under BTC.  The Government of India may contribute an 
additional amount of Rs. 250 crore per annum for a period of three years. The Ministry has stated 
that the State Government in this regard has been requested to take appropriate action to 
prioritize and identify project proposals worth Rs.1500 crore [Rs.750 crore (GoI) and Rs.750 
crore (Govt. of Assam)] under the Special Development Package (SDP) and send the list of 
proposals to be funded by MDoNER and those proposed to be funded from the State 
Governments allocation under the SDP. The requisite lists of proposals are still awaited.

4.4     Social and Infrastructure Development Fund (SIDF) 
 
4.4.1 Social and Infrastructure Development Fund (SIDF) is a one-time package. A sum of 
Rs.500 crore (subsequently enhanced to Rs.586.20 crore) was set aside for taking up 
infrastructure projects in North Eastern Region especially Arunachal Pradesh and other border 
areas facing special problems that cannot be tackled through normal schemes. It is a one-time 
package that covers projects, prioritized by the State Governments as per their requirements, 
which, inter-alia, include construction of new roads and bridges, re-establishment of new sub-
stations/transmission lines, construction/upgradation of hospitals, establishment of schools, water 
supply projects etc.  A total of 37 projects have been approved against the funds available under 
SIDF and Rs.528.70 crore has been released so far. The erstwhile NLCPR Scheme guidelines 
were adopted to select and sanction the projects under SIDF. 
 
4.4.2 The SIDF covers projects in the following sectors: - 
 
(a) Construction of new roads and bridges; 
(b) Establishment of new sub-stations/transmission lines; 
(c) Construction/Upgradation of hospitals; 
(d) Establishment of Schools; and 
(e) Water Supply Projects. 
 
4.4.3 The Ministry informed that total thirty seven (37) projects have been approved against 
the funds available under SIDF and Rs.528.70 crore has been released so far. 
 
4.4.4 Under SIDF, the projects are prioritized by State Governments as per their requirement. 
The prioritized projects received from the State Governments are placed before Project Approval 
Committee (PAC), chaired by Secretary, DoNER. After due deliberation the PAC retains the 
projects on the basis of merit. The DPR of the retained project are vetted by State Level 
Empowered Committee (SLEC) which is chaired by Chief Secretary of the respective States.  
The project vetted by SLEC is submitted to the Ministry with appraisal report for sanctioning the 
approved estimated amount and release of funds. 
 
4.4.5 The Ministry furnished the details of utilization of the allocation made during the last 
three years which are as under- 
Table 4.6 
Name of the Schemes Utilization

SIDF 44.66 69.72 19.81 
 

48 
 
4.4.6 The Ministry stated that reasons for under utilization were late receipt of project 
proposals during the financial year and non-receipt of UCs from the State Government. 
 
4.4.7 The Ministry further stated that there is no reported cost overrun in the projects 
sanctioned under SIDF. However, to avoid cost and time overruns for the projects under SIDF, 
following measures have been taken: 
(i) Progress of projects is being reviewed regularly in close coordination with concerned 
State Governments; 
(ii) Issues related to projects is also discussed during the meetings of Inter-Ministerial 
Committee wherein Chief Secretaries of NE States are member; and 
(iii) Regular visits to NE States of Chief Nodal Officers (CNO)/Nodal Officer (NO) at the 
level of Joint Secretary/Director/Deputy Secretary. 
 
4.4.8 The Committee expresses its deep displeasure at abysmal utilisation of the allocated 
fund i.e. 19.81 % upto 31 st December, 2020 in 2020-21 under the Social and Infrastructure 
Development Fund (SIDF). Keeping in view the COVID-19 Pandemic outbreak and 
various preventive steps taken by Ministry of DoNER, the Committee hopes that situation 
will improve on ground and the absorption capacity of SIDF will also increase leading to 
better utilization of funds in the Financial Year 2021-22. The Committee may be ap praised 
about the details of the projects undertaken under SIDF, status of work along with fund  
utilized for the purpose. Further, any shortfall in the fund should be appropriately taken 
up at RE stage.

CHAPTER-V 
 
PUBLIC SECTOR UNDERTAKINGS 
 
5.1 North East Regional Agricultural Marketing Corporation (NERAMAC) 
5.1.1 North Eastern Regional Agricultural Marketing Corporation (NERAMAC) Limited was 
incorporated in the year 1982 as a Government of India Enterprise under the administrative 
control of the Ministry of Development of North Eastern Region (DoNER).  
 
5.1.2 NERAMAC has been envisaged and functioning as a marketing organization dedicated to 
the agricultural sector of the North-eastern region and is lending a supporting hand to the farmers 
right from the fields and up to the markets to the end consumers for their produces. NERAMAC 
procure produces from the farmers at remunerative prices and maintain the supply chain for 
proper distribution and placing of produces in the markets throughout the country. 
 
5.1.3 The Ministry informed that during the onset of COVID-19 Pandemic, NERAMAC 
provided proactive marketing support by way of procuring products from the farmers, etc. and by 
creating stocks at their level so that the farmers in the region do not face economic challenges.  
 
5.1.4 The Ministry also informed about the details and prospective plan for expansion of 
NERAMAC activities in future which are as under- 
  
(i) Strengthening backward integration: To strengthen the backward integration, 
NERAMAC is working to on board more and more numbers of Farmer Producers 
Organisations (FPOs)/Farmers groups/Self Help Groups (SHGs)/SIGs/ FCGs with 
NERAMAC. 
 
(ii) Channelizing the forward market linkages: To channelize the forward market 
linkages, NERAMAC has proposed to tie-up with terminal markets and Mega 
Retail Stores throughout the country.  
 
(iii) Supply of quality inputs to the farmers at right price: NERAMAC shall continue 
to supply high quality inputs (seeds and planting materials) to farmers at right 
price.  
 
(iv) Revival of Non-function Processing Plants through PPP mode: NERAMAC is 
working towards revival of all its four processing plants through PPP mode. In 
this regard Integrated Ginger Processing Plant located at Byrnihat, Meghalaya is 
being revived through PPP mode.  
 
(v) Development /Marketing of processed food products: To develop/ marke t 
processed food of Agri-horti produces of NE and focus on producing new 
generation products, which is acceptable by national and international consumers 
like dehydrated fruits, fresh juice, vacuum packed fresh fruits & vegetables etc. 
 
 

50 
 
(vi) Development and promotion of organic products under Organic scheme of the 
Govt of India; NERAMAC has launched Brand „ONE‟ Organic North East for 
promotion of organic products and aims to market its organically grown produces 
under brand „ONE‟ in the coming years. 
 
(vii) Organize National & International Buyer-seller meet: NERAMAC shall continue 
to organize National & International Buyer-seller meet to bring the farmers/ 
farmers groups and entrepreneurs under one roof.  
 
(viii) Exploring innovative logistic solution NERAMAC is currently operating in a 
competitive agri-horti business for which basic infrastructure is required such as 
storage, warehouse, washing-sorting-grading-packing unit etc. NERAMAC 
proposes to set-up the basic infrastructure to enhance the value of the products for 
marketing. 
  
(ix) Serving as Implementing Agency for various schemes/programme of Govt. of 
India.  
 
(x) Geographical Indications Registration of more ethnic produces of North Eastern 
Region: NERAMAC has already facilitated the registration of 13 
produces/products of the NER under Geographical Indication (GI). NERAMAC 
shall continue to make efforts for GI registration of more such produces/products 
of NER.  
 
(xi) Capacity building of Farmers and local entrepreneurs: NERAMAC shall continue 
to organize capacity building training and awareness program for the Farmers and 
local entrepreneurs of the region.

remunerative prices to the farmers with respect to North East Regional Agricultural Market 
Corporation (NERAMAC), the Ministry informed that NERAMAC Ltd. has established the

Organizations (FPOs) and, NERAMAC Ltd. has tied up with various forward platforms/markets
