through PPP mode for NERAMAC. In respect of others, the Committee recommends that 
proactive and positive efforts may be taken to make them operational. The Committee 
takes note of the prospective plan for the expansion of NERAMAC activities in future.  The 
Committee recommends that the Ministry of DoNER in coordination with State 
Government must work out modalities for the revival and expansion of NERAMAC so that 
the Corporation is capable of carrying out its core activities i.e. creation of a world class 
infrastructure for processing, value-addition and marketing of agricultural and allied 
goods in the NER.  
5.2 North Eastern Handicrafts and Handlooms Development Corporation Limited 
(NEHHDC) 
5.2.1 The North Eastern Handicrafts and handlooms Development Corporation Ltd 
(NEHHDC) was established in March 1977 with the mandate of promotion and development of 
handicrafts and handlooms of the North Eastern Region and to provide the needed marketing 
support to the artisans and weavers of the Region. NEHHDC has been providing Marketing 
support through procurement of Handicrafts and Handlooms products directly from the Artisans, 
Weavers, Self-Help Groups at clusters and selling the same through its Sales Emporia located at 
Shillong, Guwahati, Kolkata, New Delhi and Gujarat and through Sales Promotion Offices 
located at Chennai and Bangalore and also through conduct of various exhibition and marketing 
events all over the country. 
 
5.2.2 The Ministry informed that during the onset of COVID-19 Pandemic, NEHHDC 
provided proactive marketing support by way of procuring products from the Artisans, Weavers, 
Self-Help Groups, etc. and by creating stocks at their level so that artisans of Handicrafts and 
Handlooms in the region do not face economic challenges.  
 
5.2.3 The Ministry informed that in order to achieve these broad objectives, the Corporation 
has been facing several challenges. The challenges faced by the Corporation are as under- 
 
i. NEHHDC is a small organization while mandate as per Memorandum of Association is 
large. Despite being responsible for all eight states of North East India, the company‟s 
operations are limited to Assam.   
ii. It does not have any subject matter experts (permanent / contractual) on its roll which has 
resulted in stunted growth of the organization.  
iii. It does not have automation or technical advantage.  
iv. Operations are mainly limited to procurement from vendors and there is a mismatch 
between the quantum of procurement and quantum of sale leading to low generation of 
revenue.  
v. There exists a void of the senior hierarchy resulting in shortage of staff. 
vi. Presently NEHHDC is in competition with other handicrafts and handlooms development 
agencies of the North East India State Government. 

53 
 
5.2.4 On being asked about the efforts being made by the Ministry to overcome above 
mentioned challenges, the Ministry has informed that a diagnostic study to recommend the 
Action Plan and Road-map for NEHHDC to become financially self-sustainable has been 
ordered by the Ministry and the same is being conducted by North Eastern Development Finance 
Corporation Ltd. (NEDFi). Based on the study report, Ministry will take necessary steps 
recommended to overcome the challenges given above. 
5.2.5 The following table reflects the allocation made in BE 2021-22, expenditure and 
allocation made in BE & RE 2020-21:-

5.2.6 During 2020-21, an amount of Rs. 2.00 crore was earmarked and the same amount has 
been earmarked in BE 2021-22. 
 
Progress Status of Prag Jyoti Textile Park 
 
5.2.7 On being asked about implementation status of Prag Jyoti Textile Park and Darrang 
Assam Park, the reasons for recurring delay in progress of these projects and steps taken to fix 
accountability from all concerned, the Ministry has informed that the project cost eligible for 
funding under the scheme is Rs. 47.249 crore, out of which an amount of Rs. 40.00 crore (90%) 
has been committed as Central Government Grant. Out of this Rs. 40.00 crore, Rs.12.00 crore 
has been released to the project till date. Project was supposed to be completed by March, 2018 
(3 years from the date of release of 1 st installment of Government of India grant). However, 
Special Purpose Vehicle (SPV) has submitted time to time that it has not been able to implement 
project on time as park area is a rain prone area therefore, work is halted for around 5-6 months 
per year. However, Ministry has made continuous endeavor for actively inspecting the project 
time to time and observe real time progress.  
 
5.2.8 After submission of documents for 3rd instalment, Textile commissioner office was 
asked to submit a progress report. Based upon various shortcomings in the report a visit from 
Development Commissioner (Handloom) was also requested to verify the facts.  The project was 
placed before the Project Approval Committee (PAC) on 9 th September, 2019 for review by 
Secretary (T). The proposal was put up in the PAC meeting held on December, 2019 wherein 
Special Purpose Vehicle (SPV) committed to operationalize 4-5 units by March, 2020. 
Accordingly, PAC decided to accord extension up to March, 2020 and for the release of Rs.2 
crore to the project. The funds will be spent on construction of roads, boundary walls, street 
lighting etc. Project will be reviewed in March, 2020 and further extension will be considered 
based upon the progress made. Subsequently, Rs.2.00 crore was released to the project vide 
sanction order dated 18th February, 2020.  

54 
 
 
5.2.9 Textile Commission vide its letter dated 20 th February,  2020 informed the Ministry that 
the progress on the project is standstill due to Covid-19 Pandemic. Recently, keeping in view the 
difficult scenario of construction in the region a further extension of 6 months has also been 
granted to the project vide letter dated 1 st May, 2020. The SPV informed in March, 2020 that the 
street lighting work was in full swing & was likely to be completed soon. 
 
5.2.10 The Committee notes that during the onset of Covid-19 Pandemic, North Eastern 
Handicrafts and Handlooms Development Corporation (NEHHDC) provided crucial and 
proactive support to the artisans, weavers and Self-Help Groups. The Committee is of the 
considered view that handloom and handicrafts are an integral part of the culture and  also 
a source of livelihood of people throughout the NER states. Therefore it is important to 
preserve and strengthen their rich handloom heritage. The Committee recomme nds that 
the Ministry of DoNER should provide necessary support for the formation of handloom 
and handicraft clusters which will help in inclusive development of handicrafts an d 
handloom sector in NER. This will also help in the production of superior quality produc ts, 
thereby helping the artisans to access the global market.  
 
5.2.11 The Committee also notes that Prag Jyoti Textile Park, Darrang, Assam was 
approved in the year 2014 at an estimated cost of Rs. 47.249 crore. However, the 
Committee is displeased with the slow progress made in this regard as the estimated time of 
completion was March, 2018. The Committee, therefore, recommends that reasons for 
delay must be addressed soon and the project should be closely monitored. The Comm ittee 
also recommends that the Ministry of DoNER must co-ordinate with implementing 
agencies and the line Ministries concerned to step up efforts for ensuring expeditio us 
completion of the project without further delay. 
 
***** 
  

55 
 
CHAPTER – VI 
 
SCHEMES, POLICIES AND PROGRAMMES 
 
6.1 Vision 2020 
 
6.1.1 The Committee was apprised about NER Vision 2020 document as it is a statement of the 
felt requirements and needs of the people of the North Eastern Region of India. It included 
sectoral strategies for areas such as inclusive growth, poverty eradication, rural sector, 
infrastructure, human resource development, banking, industries and tourism. It identified a six-
pronged strategy to bring about inclusive development, help alleviate poverty and ensure 
reasonable standards of living for every family. Since the adoption of the Vision 2020, NEC has 
referred to the document while planning the various developmental strategies of the NER. 
 
6.1.2 The Ministry, in its reply to the questionnaire, informed the Committee  about various 
projects for maximising self-governance and participatory planning under NER Vision 
2020. The NEC‟s flagship project, the North Eastern Regio n Community Resource Management 
Project for Upland Areas (NERCORMP) was established to improve the livelihood of vulnerable 
groups in a sustainable manner. NERCORMP operates in four states covering ten districts viz: 
Assam (KarbiAnglong and Dima Hasao), Manipur (Ukhrul, Senapati, Chaurachandpur and 
Chandel), Meghalaya (West Garo Hills and West Khasi Hills) and Arunachal Pradesh (undivided 
Tirap including Longding and Changlang). The project has covered a total of 2640 villages in 
these four states benefitting 1,18,843 households. The Project has also supported formation of 
8,326 Self Help Groups (SHGs) and 2,960 Natural Resource Management Groups (NaRMGs). 
Through the support from NERCORMP, these Community Based Organisations (CBOs) are 
working together with all the community members to bring about a sustainable livelihood for the 
village and society as a whole. The present phase of the project will be completed by 31 st March, 
2021.
 
 
6.1.3 Accelerating agricultural growth for rural development was also carried out under 
NER Vision 2020. Various initiatives for promotion of horticulture and floriculture, medicinal 
and aromatic plants and herbs including organic farming are taken to capture highly 
remunerative niche markets abroad. Interventions have been made in Animal Husbandry and 
Fisheries to increase farm-based production and meet the supply gap in the Region. Replacement 
of traditional cropping patterns especially Jhum Cultivation by short-duration, high-yielding 
crops and promotion of bamboo and rubber plantations for better incomes have also been carried 
out. NERCORMP has also provided better crop technology and micro-credit in hill States.
 
 
6.1.4 The Ministry also updated the Committee about developing sectors with co mparative 
advantage under Vision 2020. NEC has achieved significant progress in some of these areas: 
 
a. Tea processing - During the financial year 2011-12, NEC has supported a project on 
"Enhancement of Capacity of the Tea Processing Factory at Tebaria, West Tripura" at an  
estimated cost of Rs. 498.39 lakhs and “Expansion o f Temi Tea Estate in Gangtok, 
Sikkim” during 2015-16. 

56 
 
b. Establishment of Industrial Estates, Common Facility Centres to address infrastructure 
bottleneck that have been supported by the NEC in various states and are progressing 
satisfactorily. 
 
c. Promote clust er-approach development of Industry based on locally available raw 
materials; NEC has actively supported the Schemes of the NE Handicrafts and Handloom 
Development Corporation, the NE Regional Agricultural Marketing Corporation. 
 
d. NEC has identified much needed skills in the Hospitality and Tourism Industry and 
funded capacity building of the local youths in these areas through reputed Institutes like 
the World Class Skills Training Centre at Delhi and the Indian Institute of 
Entrepreneurship, Guwahati. 
 
6.1.5 The Committee was appraised about various human development parameters 
including health. These parameters include areas such as educational in frastructure, 
particularly in technical areas such as engineering, information technology  and bio-
technology, hostels, skills training and coaching interventions. It also visualises promotion 
of social interaction of NER people with the rest of the country. More than a  tenth of the 
NEC‟s resources are targeted towards building up of the human resources in th e regio n. 
Educational and sports infrastructure including hostels form a major componen t of 
expenditure in this area. Specifically, some of NEC‟s achievements in this area a re given 
below:- 
a. Barooah Cancer Institute, started with the support of NEC, was recently handed ov er 
to the Department of Atomic Energy (DAE) and Tata Memorial Centre  for more 
focussed and specialised interventions. 
 
b. There are 18 on-going projects of infrastructure, equipments etc. for the healthc are 
services in government health centres, Medical Colleges, District/Civil Hospitals etc. 
 
c. A number of projects have been funded for construction of tribal hostels, teach er‟s 
quarters, college buildings, science labs, school auditorium etc. which are 
progressing satisfactorily. 
 
d. Scholarships for students studying in technical courses are sponsored by NEC. Th ese 
scholarships are disbursed to the States of the NER. 
 
e. A number of employment oriented training programmes on various courses are being 
sponsored by NEC e.g. Computer Software, Computer Hardware, Business Process 
Outsourcing (BPO), Banana Fibre Extraction, Beauty and Hair care, Rural 
Development,  Computer-aided design (CAD)/ Computer-aided manufacturing 
(CAM)/ Computer Numerical Control (CNC) Technology & Machine operation, 
Ayurvedic Therapy, Hearing Disability, Adventure Sports, Sports Coaches, Gems 
and Jewellery making etc., coaching for competitive exam such as UPSC, SSC and  
Bank POs, Retailed Management, CAT/MAT etc. 
 
f. In the field of sports, number of national and regional sports tournaments are h eld in 
NE States, sports infrastructure and cash reward are given under “NEC Chairman‟s 
Sports Award for Excellence in International & National Sports Meets by Sportsmen/ 
Sportswomen of NE‟ by NEC. 

57 
 
g. To encourage and increase inter-cultural adventure activities, NEC h ad also 
supported a programme titled “Youth to the Edge” where youth from all ove r India 
camped and toured the North East of India. 
 
h. A North East Cultural Centre named Centre for North East Culture (CNEC) was set 
up at CCRT campus, Dwarka in 24th January, 2012. 
 
i. As recommended by the M P Bezbaruah Committee, a North East Convention Centre 
with facilities for a Museum, Library, Exhibitions, Seminars, Ethnic Food Courts, 
Cultural performances and limited guest accommodation has been sanctioned in 
November 2020 at Sector 13, Dwarka, New Delhi at an estimated cost of Rs 116.38 
crore. 
 
6.1.6 The Ministry informed the Committee about creation of an investment climate by 
providing state-of-the-art infrastructure, especially connectivity. Vision 2020 stressed a lot on 
strengthening infrastructure and connectivity in the Region. It visua lizes better air and water 
connectivity with the rest of the country and the neighbouring countries. 
a. More than 45% of the NEC‟s annual expenditure is dedicated towards provid ing 
infrastructure such as roads, bridges and airports. NEC has funded various projects 
across all sectors of the economy of the North Eastern States. Construction of 10,500 kms 
of roads, installation of 694 MW of power generating capacity and 2540 kms of 
transmission and distribution lines have been carried out with NEC funding. 
 
b. There are total 13 operational Airports in the region. Further, two airports are  ready 
for operations and one new Greenfield Airport is also under construction. 
 
c. The project of connecting all State Capitals by rail is being taken up by th e Ministry 
of Railways. Connectivity with Bangladesh through rail is being supported by the 
DoNER Ministry. 
 
d. NEC‟s major sector is infrastructure and in the road sector alone, in the last five 
years NEC has been able to complete 977.74 kms of roads. 
 
6.1.7 Generating public and private investment for development of the NER, the Vision 2020 
document stipulated that investment climate in the NE Region should be improved to attract 
public and private investment and also encourage states to generate resources from their own tax 
and non-tax resources. 
 
a. Ministry of DoNER monitors the spending of various Ministries of the 10% of their 
budget in the NE Region and recommends re-allocation of funds between Ministries. 
 
b. NEC in its role as a think tank, partners with various trade bodies in organizing 
workshops, symposia and seminars to highlight the strengths of the NE and attract 
investments from the Government agencies and private bodies. 
 
6.1.8 The Committee notes that North Eastern Region Community Resource 
Management Project for Upland Areas (NERCORMP) is operating in four States covering 
ten districts viz: Assam, Manipur, Meghalaya and Arunachal Pradesh. The project has 
covered a total 2640 villages in these four States supporting formation of 8326 Self Help 
Groups (SHGs) and 2,960 Natural Resource Management Groups (NaRMGs) benefitting 

58 
 
1,18,843 households. The Committee recommends that the NERCORMP may be extended 
to the other districts in the North-Eastern Region. The Committee also recommend s that 
the Ministry may periodically monitor ongoing projects under NER Vision 2020 for their 
completion within the stipulated time frame. 
 
6.1.9 The Committee notes that the Ministry has proposed to cosntruct the North-East 
Convention Centre at Sector 13, Dwarka, New Delhi at the estimated cost of Rs.116.38 
crore for which funds have been sanctioned in November 2020. The Committee would  like 
to be apprised about the status of the project. 
 
6.2 Creating new and improving existing infrastructure in NER  
 
6.2.1 The Ministry of DoNER apprised the Committee that various initiatives were taken by 
MDoNER to create new and improve existing infrastructure in NER. Some of them are as 
follows: 
a. North Eastern Special Infrastructure Scheme (NESIDS) was launched on 15 th 
December, 2017 as a Central Sector Scheme with 100% central funding under which 
financial assistance to NER States are provided to fill up the gaps in specified sectors 
of (a) physical infrastructure relating to water supply, power and connectivity to 
enhance tourism and (b) social sectors for creation of infrastructure in the areas of 
primary and secondary sectors of education and health. 
 
b. Hill Area Development Programme (HADP), a pilot Scheme launched in 2017-18 for 
holistic and inclusive development of hill districts of Tamenglong and Noney in 
Manipur. It strives to strengthen the State‟s efforts in addressing the physical and 
social infrastructural gaps in its remote hilly districts and provides for technology 
driven enabling eco-system for delivery of public services. It also envisages 
harnessing the potential of identified local resources/natural endowments to improve 
production and value addition for enhancement of income of local people. 
  
c. The Ministry of DoNER has sanctioned 45 projects and released Rs.197.83 crore in 
2019-20 as one time central assistance for re-building totally washed away/damaged 
infrastructure during floods in 2017 in the North Eastern Region. 
  
d. NEC under North East Road Sector Development Schemes has sanctioned 21 projects 
worth Rs.1204.61 crore for upgradation of orphan roads in NER since 2014-15.  As 
on 31st December, 2020 an amount of Rs.436.19 crore has been released. 
 
6.3 Status of Major Roads 
 
6.3.1 The Committee sought to know the status and timeline of road construction under North 
Eastern Roads Investment Program (NESRIP). The Ministry informed the Committee that out of 
12 road projects, 6 Road projects have been completed which includes 5 road projects in the 
State of Assam viz Kalitakuchi to Barpeta (AS-37C); Bilasipara to Fakiragram (AS-11); 
Tamulpur to Paneri (AS-02); Paneri to Udalguri (AS-03); One Major bridge (AS-02-03) and one 
road in Meghalaya Garobada to Dalu (MLN-1).  

59 
 
6.3.2 The Committee was further informed that the Scheme is scheduled for completion by 
August, 2021 and all efforts are being made to complete the remaining projects by the said 
deadline. 
 
6.3.3 On being asked to furnish progress report on construction, renovation and widening of 
various NER road development projects and how much area has been covered by the 
construction of roads in the NER in the last five years, the Committee was informed that North 
East Road Sector Development Scheme (NERSDS) is the only specialized road sector Scheme 
under MDoNER which deals with the renovation/widening of existing roads in NER.  Under this 
Scheme, total 21 projects totaling a length of 441.87 km worth Rs.1429.67 crore have been 
sanctioned by NEC as on 31 st January, 2021 for 8 States of NER. Out of these 2 projects have 
been completed and 19 Projects are at various stages of implementation. 
 
6.3.4 The Ministry furnished lists of  National Highway projects implemented by State PWD, 
NHIDCL and NHAI in NER, which are as per ANNEXURE I, ANNEXURE II  and  
ANNEXURE III respectively. 
 
6.3.5 The Committee notes that the progress of road construction projects have been 
affected by serious adversities like hilly terrain, inclement weather conditions an d medical 
emergencies like COVID-19 Pandemic. The Committee, recommends that th e Ministry 
must conduct special drives for completion of these projects as they have been initiated way 
back in 2013. Considering the deadline of August 2021, the Committee recommends that 
the Ministry of DoNER should form an empowered team of senior officers from the  
Ministries of DoNER and Road Transport and Highways to visit the project sites. The 
Committee further recommends that the Ministry may setup an accountability mechanism 
for poor and non-performance and apprise the Committee about the action taken in thi s 
regard.   
 
 
6.4 Special Accelerated Road Development Programme for North East (SARDPNE) 
 
6.4.1 Special Accelerated Road Development Programme for North East (SARDP-NE) was 
formulated by Ministry of Road Transport & Highways (MoRTH) during 2005-06 with a view to 
improve the existing National Highways network in the region besides providing connectivity to 
all District Headquarters by 2-lane road with the nearest National Highway and also to provide 
inter-state connectivity, connectivity with neighbouring countries and strategic roads.  
 
6.4.2 The Committee sought to know the latest status of Special Accelerated Road 
Development NE (SARDP-NE) Programme. In its reply, the Ministry has informed that  
Government of India has given approval for 2/4 laning of 6418 km of various categories of roads 
under Phase „A‟ and Arunachal Package of SARDP-NE in North East at an estimated investment 
of about Rs 30,450 crore. Apart from the above, Government of India has also given approval for 
preparation of detailed project report for 3723 km of roads under Phase „B‟ of SARDP -NE. As 
of now 5601 Km of length has been sanctioned under SARDP-NE (including Arunachal Pradesh 
Package) out of which 3483 Km has been completed. 
 

60 
 
6.5 Status of Electricity Transmission and Distribution 
 
6.5.1 The Ministry informed the Committee that there are two important projects being 
implemented in NER for strengthening of electricity transmission and distribution network in the 
North Eastern Region. Details are given below: 
 
(i) North Eastern Region Power System Improvement Project (NERPSIP) - The project was 
approved in December, 2014 with initial sanctioned cost of Rs. 5111.33 crore which has been 
revised to Rs. 6700 crore in December, 2020.  As on 28 th January, 2021 Rs. 3010.33 crore has 
been released.  The project covers six States, i.e., Assam, Manipur, Mizoram, Meghalaya, 
Nagaland and Tripura. Works in all awarded packages is under progress. Financial progress 
37.6% against RE as on 28 th January, 2021. The construction works in the States are in progress 
and 1145.05/5856 MVA addition has been done till 31 st January, 2021, (i.e., Assam 600 MVA, 
Meghalaya 105 MVA, Manipur 238.55 MVA, Nagaland 140 MVA, Tripura 61.5 MVA).  The 
target completion date has also been revised from December, 2018 to December, 2021. 
 
(ii) Comprehensive Scheme for strengthening Power Transmission and Distribution System 
in Arunachal Pradesh and Sikkim - The Scheme was approved in October, 2014 with 
estimated cost of Rs. 4754.42 crore and Rs. 2564.24 crore have been released till January, 2021.  
Expenditure Finance Committee (EFC) has recommended revision of estimated cost to Rs. 
9129.32 crore with the completion date for 204 elements as December, 2021.  Works in all 
awarded packages is under progress. The construction works in the States are in progress and the 
financial progress is 32.1% in Arunachal Pradesh and 62.7% in Sikkim. 
 
6.5.2 On being asked about the working capacity of Inter State Transmission System (ISTS), 
the Ministry informed that the ISTS and transformation capacity through Extra High Voltage 
(EHV) sub-stations are adequate to meet the current peak demand as well as the NEP projected 
demand at the end of 2021-22. There is adequate inter-regional transmission capacity for North 
Eastern-North and North Eastern-East is 3000 MW and 2860 MW respectively.  Following Inter-
State Transmission System (ISTS)  have been completed in 2020:- 
(i) 400/220 kV, 2x500 MVA S/S at New Kohima, Nagaland. 
(ii) Imphal – New Kohima 400 kV D/C line 
(iii) New Kohima – New Mariani 400 kV D/C line  
 
Following work under North Eastern Region Strengthening Scheme (NERSS)-II are nearing 
completion:- 
 
(i) Establishment of 400/132 kV, 2x315 MVA S/S at Surajmaninagar 
(ii) Establishment of 400/132 kV, 2x315 MVA S/S at P.K. Bari 
(iii) Surajmaninagar – P.K. Bari 400 kV D/C line (Twin ACSR Moose) 
(iv) AGTPP (NEEPCO) – P.K. Bari (TSECL) 132 kV D/C line 
(v) Biswanath Chariali – Itanagar 132 kV D/C line 
(vi) Silchar – Misa 400 kV D/C line 
 
 
 

61 
 
6.6 Civil Aviation 
 
6.6.1 The Committee sought to know the measures being taken to operationalize the non-
operational airports in the NER.  In response the Committee was informed that the non 
operational Airports in NER are being made operational under relevant Schemes of Ministry of 
Civil Aviation such as revival of unserved and underserved airports and RCS- UDAN subject to 
funds availability. 
 
6.6.2 The Committee also sought to know how many unserved Airports/Airstrips in the NER 
are being taken up under UDAN-RCS Scheme of Ministry of Civil Aviation and asked to furnish 
details such as allocated funds and current status. In response, the Ministry has stated that to 
augment the RCS-UDAN, Cabinet Committee on Economic Affairs has approved the proposal 
for revival of existing unserved/underserved airports/airstrips of the State Governments, Airports 
Authority of India, Public Sector Undertakings and Civil Enclaves. An airport which is included 
in the awarded routes of RCS–UDAN and requires upgradation/development for commencement 
of RCS operations are developed under the "Revival of unserved and underserved airports” 
Scheme. The Committee was informed that at present, 16 airports/airstrips/heliports/ 
helipads/water aerodromes have been awarded under the Scheme and are being developed in 
NER. The Ministry also furnished the details of allocated funds and current status of the 
Schemes undergoing in NER under RCS-UDAN as under:-

6.6.5 The Ministry has further stated that the Airport Projects are delayed on account of 
bottlenecks such as land acquisition issues, shifting of utilities and settlement, etc. The Inter-
Ministerial Committee on Civil Aviation was constituted in order to fast-track the development 
of infrastructure projects on airports in NER. IMC-Civil Aviation also envisages greater synergy 
in ongoing efforts in the development of the aviation sector in NER and review issues involved 
in the implementation of Schemes/programmes. Since the day of the constitution of IMC-Civil 
Aviation, 6 IMC meetings have been held. The meeting with all concerned stakeholders such as 
the Airport Authority of India (AAI), Ministry of Civil Aviation, State Governments in NER, etc. 
enables to identify the bottlenecks for resolution with further follow up with concerned State and 
Central agencies for resolution. 
 
6.6.6 The Committee notes the projects undertaken by the Ministry of Civil Aviation and 
hopes that these ongoing/new projects when completed would improve the air connectivity 
and operational efficiency of Civil Aviation in NER. The Committee desires that the 
Ministry of DoNER should coordinate with the Ministry of Civil Aviation and the State 
Governments concerned for better synergy and removal of the bottlenecks in  the 
implementation of the projects undertaken. It also recommends that the ongoing projects 
be completed in the stipulated time frame and without cost overrun. 
 
6.7 Waterways 
 
6.7.1 The Committee sought to know about the various ongoing waterways development 
projects in NER and their progress status. In response, the Ministry has furnished the details 
regarding ongoing waterway development projects in NER, which is as under:- 
(i) Development of National Waterway-2 (River Brahmaputra; length 891 km) Sadia to 
Bangladesh Border. 

66 
 
(ii) National Waterway-16 (NW16) (River Barak; length 121 km) Bhanga-Lakhipur 
stretch including Indo-Bangladesh Protocol (IBP) route. 
 
6.7.2 The Ministry also furnished the details of ongoing waterways development projects in 
NER and their status, which are as under:- 
 
I. National Waterway-16 (NW16) (River Barak; length 121 km) Bhanga-Lakhipur stretch 
including IBP route: 
River Barak was declared as National Waterway-16 (NW-16) in the year 2016. It connects 
Silchar, Karimganj and Badarpur in the Cachar valley of Assam with Haldia and Kolkata ports 
through Indo-Bangladesh Protocol (IBP) Route. The facilities created and planned for NW-16 
& Indo-Bangladesh Protocol at a cost of Rs 145 crore for a duration of 5 years (2020-2025) a re 
as under: 
a. Fairway development:  Dredging, for fairway maintenance with Least Available 
Depth (LAD) along with navigation aids between Ujjainigram to Bhanga was 
commenced in Nov 2017.   
b. Sonamura-Daudkandi stretch was included as Indo-Bangladesh Protocol and 
Sonamura was declared as Port of Call.  Inland Waterways Authority of India (IWAI) 
in consultation with the Govt. of Tripura (GoT) and Land Ports Authority of India 
(LPAI) has constructed a floating jetty adjacent to the Srimantapur LCS in July‟2020.  
c. Navigational Aids:  At present, day navigational aids have been provided and is 
maintained in the Bhanga to Sridharpur stretch of River Barak. 
 
II. Development of National Waterway-2 (River Brahmaputra; length 891 km) Sadia to 
Bangladesh Border 
River Brahmaputra from Dhubri (Bangladesh Border) to Sadiya (891 km) was declared as 
National Waterway-2 (NW-2) in 1988. The waterway is being developed with a fairway of 
required depth and width, day and night navigation aids and terminals. The progress of links 
planned at a cost of Rs 461 crore for a duration of 5 years (2020-2025) are as under: 
a. Fairway development works/ River conservancy works:  
Annual river conservancy works like bundling and maintenance dredging are carried out 
regularly to maintain the navigable fairway. 
b. Permanent Terminals: Two permanent terminals, i.e., the multi-modal terminal at Pandu 
and Ro-Ro terminal with a sloping jetty (which can be used for Ro-Ro operations as well 
as normal cargo loading and unloading by cranes) along with other facilities at Dhubri 
have been completed and are operational. 
c. Floating Terminals: Floating terminals have been provided at eleven locations viz. 
Hatsingimari, Dhubri, Jogighopa, Tezpur, Silghat, Biswanathghat, Neamati, Sengajan, 
Bogibil, Dibrugarh/Oakland and Oriumghat. 
d. Aids to navigation:  The entire channel has been marked with day navigation marks. 
Night navigation facilities comprising of solar operated lights on beacon have also been 
provided and maintained between Dhubri and Silghat (440 km). Differential Global 
Positioning System (DGPS) stations are provided at four locations viz. Dhubri, 
Jogighopa, Biswanthghat and Dibrugarh for safe navigation of vessels with electronic 
charts.  
 

67 
 
6.7.3 The Committee sought to know the detailed status of the Integrated National Waterways 
Programme along with details of fund allocation, utilization and completion status of the same. 
In response, the Ministry has informed that the Inland Waterways Authority of India (IWAI) 
under the Ministry of Ports, Shipping & Waterways has no specific Integrated National 
Waterways Programme (INWP).

III. NLCPR (Central) Project for Flood & Erosion Control: Protection of Majuli Island 
from flood and erosion of river Brahmaputra  “Protection of Majuli Island from flood and 
erosion of river Brahmaputra funded by Ministry of DoNER in Majuli District of Assam”. The 
Scheme with an estimated cost of Rs. 233.57 crore envisages for protection of the southern

estimated cost of Rs. 233.57 crore, an amount of Rs. 207.00 crore is being funded by 
MoDoNER, Govt. of India. 
 
6.8.3 The Committee notes that there are 9 on-going flood control projects under 
NLCPR. The Committee is aware that every year flood causes havoc in NER and timely 
implementation of all flood control projects is critical to the development of the Region. 
The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Ministry should ensure timely completion 
of all pending flood control projects without cost overruns.   
 
6.9 Drinking water supply 
 
6.9.1 The Ministry was asked to furnish the details of projects related to drinking water supply 
such as piped water supply proposed under the current budget in NER along with the financia l 
allocation for it. In reply to this, the Committee was informed that in the current budget under 
NESIDS 2020-21, one project "Greater Baghmara Water Supply Scheme" with the estimated 
cost of Rs 47.54 crore has been sanctioned on 27 th January, 2021. The total population to be 
benefitted from the water supply Scheme is 63,335. So, taking a family size of 5 in Meghalaya, 
approx 13,000 households will be covered under the Scheme. The time frame for completion of 
the project is 2 years from the date of sanction. 
 
6.10 Kaladan Multimodal Transit Transport (KMTT) Project 
 
6.10.1   The Committee was informed that in accordance with the Act East Policy of the 
Government, the Kaladan Multimodal Transit Transport (KMTT) Project was conceptualized by 
the Ministry of External Affairs (MEA) to provide alternative connectivity to Mizoram with 
Haldia/Kolkata ports through the Kaladan River in Myanmar. It is a strategic connectivity project 
to connect the eastern ports of India with Myanmar by developing a port at Sittwe on the western 
coast of Myanmar and linking the Sittwe port with a river channel and road to Mizoram. The 
project has two components: (i) the Waterways component and (ii) the Roadways component.  
 
6.10.2   The Waterways component of KMTT includes the construction of a Port and an Inland 
Water Transport (IWT) Terminal at Sittwe; dredging of the access channel and river channel at  
the Port, development of 158 Km long navigational channel along Kaladan River from Sittwe to 
Paletwa; construction of an IWT Terminal at Palewa and construction of six 300-ton barges. M/s 
Inland Waterways Authority of India (IWAI) is Project Development Consultant (PDC) since 
March 2009. The Port and IWT development contract was carried out by M/s Essar Projects 
India Ltd. (EPIL) since April 2010. The construction of the main work was completed in May 
2017 and handed over to the Government of Myanmar. Additional works such as the 
construction of staff quarters, fuel bunkering, etc. at Paletwa, procurement of furniture, removal 
of 2 wrecks found in the access channel at Sittwe committed by the Government of India were 
also completed in December 2018. A port operator has been appointed jointly by the 

73 
 
Government of India and Myanmar in December 2019. The port operator has commenced its 
operation from 1st February 2020. 
 
6.10.3   Efforts are being made to operationalize the port and IWT terminals. A project oversight 
Committee (POC) has been constituted with members from MEA, IWAI and Kolkata Port Trust 
to oversee the day-to-day activities involved in the early operationalization of Sittwe Port.  
 
6.10.4   The Road component of KMTT includes a new greenfield road project of 109.2 km to 
be built from Paletwa in Myanmar to Zorinpui on the India-Myanmar border (Mizoram) to 
double-lane NH specifications. M/s. IRCON Infrastructure & Services Limited (IRCONISL) is 
the Project Management Consultant. The work was awarded to EPI-C&C JV [a joint venture of 
Engineers Projects (India) Limited and C&C Construction Co. Ltd.] on 17 th March 2017. The 
current physical progress is 3.78% as on 31st January 2021. 
 
6.10.5   In the meeting held on 23 rd February, 2021, The Committee inquired about the 
challenges that have hampered the progress of the KMTT project. The Ministry replied that the 
execution of the KMTT project, especially the road component, has faced many challenges due 
to security problems, ongoing insurgency and remoteness of the site. Major challenges being 
faced are:  
a. Continuous insurgency presence in the region is delaying the implementation of the 
projects. Construction of a road from Paletwa in Chin State to Zorinpui in India‟s 
Mizoram State has been impeded by the precarious security situation in the region. 
b. The initial plan of the project was to develop a 225 km long waterway from Sittwe Port 
till Kaletwa (transshipment point) and a road component of 62 km from Kaletwa up to 
Zorinpui in Mizoram at the Indo-Myanmar border. This was subsequently changed due to 
actual geographical conditions on the site to 158 km of waterways and 109 km of the 
surface route after shifting the trans-shipment point on the Kaladan River from Kaletwato 
Paletwa and the specifications of the road component were also changed from 
intermediate to double lane as well. The shifting of trans-shipment point and changes in 
specifications and scope of work along with additional facilities led to the revision of the 
cost and timelines. 
c. One of the JV partners of the appointed contractor (M/s C&C constructions) has gone 
bankrupt and an insolvency proceeding has been initiated in the NCLT since March 2019 
due to the acute financial crisis of the contractor. 
d. Prolong monsoon season in Myanmar (May to November). 
e. Completely greenfield nature of the project and difficult terrain along the project length 
in Myanmar.  
f. Due to the Novel COVID-19 Pandemic, the Government of Myanmar has implemented 
strict restrictions on the movement of personnel and machines which have affected the 
access and pace of the work.  
 
6.10.6   The Ministry further informed that the revised project cost approved by the Cabinet in 
October 2015 is Rs. 2904.04 crore (Rs 982.99 crore for waterways and Rs.1921.05 crore for road 
component). Funds released so far for the Kaladan Road component are Rs. 118 crore.  Funds 
spent on developing the Kaladan waterways component are Rs. 503 crore.      
 

74 
 
6.10.7 The Committee takes note of the progress of the Kaladan Multimodal Transit 
Transport (KMMTT) project that is being piloted and funded by the Ministry of Ext ernal 
Affairs. The Committee expresses its displeasure with the physical progress of the road 
construction work. The Committee has taken cognizance of the problems faced in the 
execution of the KMMTT project, specifically the road component and hopes that the work 
on the road component of the KMMTT project is expedited. The Committee, ther efore, 
recommends that the reasons for the delay in the execution of the KMMTT project must be 
addressed and rectified soon and the project is closely monitored and reviewed freque ntly 
for its
 early completion.  
 
6.11 Tourism 
 
6.11.1   The Ministry informed that with the objective to highlight the tourism potential of the 
North Eastern Region of India, the Ministry of Tourism in association with the North Eastern 
States has been organizing the International Tourism Mart (ITM) on a rotation basis since the 
year 2013. ITM is a 3-day event. So far a total of 7 ITM‟S have been held in various states of the 
North Eastern Region. 
 
6.11.2   NLCPR Scheme was discontinued on 15 th December, 2017 for any fresh sanction and a 
new central sector Scheme NESIDS has replaced the NLCPR Scheme. A total of 453 projects 
against the total sanctioned cost of Rs 6910.82 crore are ongoing under NLCPR. The state-wise 
details of the sanctioned project and fund released in 2019-20 & 2020-21 follows:-

6.11.3 Details of ongoing projects during 2019-20 and 2020-21 under NEC are given in

6.11.4 The Committee sought to know the steps taken to d evelop niche tourism/high-end 
tourism in the NER.  In response, the Ministry  informed that the Ministry of Tourism has 
given financial assistance to build a golf course in Mizoram. Various components are being 
supported under Swadesh Darshan Scheme. Ministry of Tourism also supports tea tourism. 
Further, the Ministry of Tourism vigorously promotes Sustainable Tourism in India Criteria and 
Indicators (STCI) for eco-tourism in a responsible sustainable way through its various 
stakeholder. NEC has taken up projects as per the proposals and priority lists sent by State 

75 
 
Governments of the North East. However, the prospect of developing high-end tourism is being 
encouraged in the NE States. 
 
6.11.5 On being asked if the North Eastern States foresee educational tourism as a strategy for 
development and social change, the Ministry replied that the Indian Institute of Tourism & 
Travel Management (IITTM), under the Ministry of Tourism has opened its camp at Shillong in 
the year 2018. The tour guide, training programs for Meghalaya are being organized in the camp. 
The efforts are being made to attract and nominate candidates from the other States of NER for 
the 'Hunar Se RozgarTak' (HSRT) Programme. Once the camp is converted into Centre, reg ular 
educational programmes like MBA (TTM) and BBA (T&T) shall be organized by IITTM. At 
present, the requirement of NER candidates is catered by the IITTM, Bhubaneswar centre. The 
growing popularity of attaining knowledge and technical competencies from the North East 
creative and suitable atmosphere has resulted in an increase in education tourism which has 
contributed to the development and social change. The roadmap to achieve its full potential in 
this area is being worked out at different levels and platforms. 
 
6.11.6  The Committee further sought to know the steps that were taken for placing Shillong and 
Gangtok as global tourist attractions. In response to that, the Committee was informed that the 
Ministry of Tourism supported various golf events in the North East States by various organizers 
viz. organized at Shilling, Gangtok and Mizoram. Ministry of Tourism has also supported the 
global film festival organized in Sikkim. As far as the Ministry of North Eastern Region is 
concerned, the project locations are identified by the respective State Governments of the North 
Eastern States. 
 
6.11.7 The Ministry further informed that with the objective to highlight the tourism potential of 
the North Eastern Region of India, the Ministry of Tourism in association with the North Eastern 
States has been organizing the International Tourism Mart (ITM) on a rotation basis since the 
year 2013. ITM is a 3-day event. So far a total of 7 ITM‟S have been held in various states of the 
North Eastern Region. The host States assist the Ministry of Tourism by way of hosting some of 
the arrangements, for the event, however, the major expenses on board and lodging, airfare, 
ground transportation for the foreign delegates, domestic delegates, various officials are borne by 
the MoT. Ministry of Tourism has been engaging the services of “Ashok Events” which is a 
division of India Tourism Development Corporation (ITDC) as the event manager for making 
the logistic arrangements viz, setting up of the venue, stalls/booth, stage, A/V, decoration, the 
exhibition area for local handlooms and handicrafts and organizing of cultural performance of 
North Eastern States during the 3-day event. As a result of these marts, the foreign tourist visits 
to this region have increased to double-digit figures. The Compounded Annual Growth Rate 
(CAGR) during 2013-2018 was 24.04%. The expenditure incurred in the previous 8 editions of 
the ITM is given below:-

6.11.8 The Ministry informed that the major activities undertaken during the ITM are as 
follows:-  
a)   The Ministry of Tourism invites foreign delegates from different countries under the 
Hospitality Scheme of the Travel Industry and Media to participate in the International 
Tourism Marts. 
b)   In addition to the foreign delegates, a large number of domestic delegates representing 
the travel industry also participate in the event. 
c)   During the ITM, B2B meetings are held between the foreign delegates and domestic 
tourism service providers from other parts of the country i.e. as buyers and sellers. 
d)   The Tourism Departments of the 8 North Eastern States make Power-Point Presentations 
about the tourism potential and attractions of their respective states to the foreign 
delegates as well as the domestic tourism Service providers from other parts of the 
country. 
e)   The Tourism Departments of the 8 North Eastern States also set up stalls/ booths to 
exhibit the handicrafts of their respective states. 
f)    The 8 North Eastern States organizes post-Familiarization (FAM) Tours for the foreign 
delegates to experience the unique flora, fauna and cultural attractions of the North East 
Region. 
g)   A cultural program is organized with the help of the North East Zone Cultural Centre 
(NEZCC) to display the various folk dance forms of the 8 North Eastern States to the 
foreign and domestic delegates. 
h)   Dinners are hosted by the Ministry as well as the host State wherein the local cuisine is 
also prepared to be flavored and savored by the foreign and domestic delegates. 
 
6.11.9 In the meeting held on 23 rd February, 2021, the Committee sought to know the status of 
fund allocation to „Destination North East‟. The Ministry replied that Destination North East is 
an annual festival organized by the DONER with the objective of showcasing and promoting the 
various tourism sectors including art & craft, textiles, ethnic products, etc. not only for the 
tourists but also investors and entrepreneurs, national and international. It also aims at taking the 
North East Region to other parts of the country and bringing them closer together for better 
understanding and strengthening national integration. Previous editions of the festival were held 
in Delhi, Chandigarh and Varanasi (2019). The focus of Destination North East 2020, held 
virtually was on the theme “The Emerging  Delightful Destinations”. Total expenditure on 
Destination North East held in 2020 is Rs.1.28 crore. 
6.11.10 The Committee further inquired about the steps taken by the MDoNER to prevent racial 
discrimination against NE students outside NER. The Ministry furnished the details as follows:- 
 In pursuance of Supreme Court‟s Order dt. 14 th December, 2016 in WP (C) 103/2014 – 
Karma Dorgee and Ors. Vs UOI &Ors, a Monitoring Committee has been set up in MHA under 
the Chairmanship of JS (NE) with two members of NE region namely Dr. Alana Golmei General 

77 
 
Secretary, Northeast Support Centre & Helpline and Dr. MizumNyodu, Assistant Professor, 
Govt. Law College, Arunachal Pradesh to monitor the redressal of issues relating to racial 
discrimination faced by North East people including students residing in different parts of the 
country. The meeting of the Monitoring Committee is being held regularly. Further, an e-mail 
i.d. has been created [ne.complaints@mha.gov.in] by MHA for receiving 
complaints/suggestions/grievances of North Eastern people, including students, who claim to be 
victims or have faced any act of racial abuse/racial atrocities/racial violence/racial discrimination 
in any part of the country. So far six complaints have been received on this e-mail i.d. in M HA 
on which necessary action has been taken.  
 Further, MDoNER/NEC is also working towards the welfare of NE Students.  The 
following three projects have been sanctioned keeping in view the safety and welfare of NE 
Students: 
a. North Eastern Council Information/Convention Centre at Dwarka, New Delhi: With a 
view to promote and share common interests of NE people, administrative approval for 
an amount Rs.116.38 has been issued on 3rd November, 2020 for the construction of NEC 
Information & Convention Centre in Dwarka, Delhi.  An amount of Rs.6.43 crore has 
already been released to DDA for allotment of land for construction of the said centre.   
b. Construction of Barak Hostel for the NE Students at Jawaharlal Nehru University, New 
Delhi:  For facilitating the stay of NE students in Delhi, MDoNER/NEC is constructing a 
Barak Hostel for the NE Students at Jawaharlal Nehru University, New Delhi at a cost of 
Rs.26.38 crore out of which Rs.11.01 crore has already been released.  Approximately, 
30% work of the hostel has been completed as of date. 
c. Construction of Hostel for the students of North East States at  Rohini, Delhi. An amount 
of Rs.2.49 cr for purchase of land and Rs.48.33 cr. for construction of boundary wall has 
been sanctioned by NEC.  Delhi University is being consulted for its views on running 
the said proposed hostel.   
6.11.11 The Committee notes the steps taken in the  direction of preventing racial 
discrimination of North Eastern Students in the rest of the country. It strongly condemns 
such acts and recommends that a PAN India awareness campaign should be carried out for 
improved integration and understanding of North Eastern culture in the rest of the 
country. The Committee further recommends that the administrative and judicial 
mechanism should be strengthened for the protection of such individuals.  
6.11.12   On being asked about the acquisition, preserving and development of INA 
Headquarters at Moirang, Manipur as a heritage site by the Government, the Ministry replied that 
in the 69 th Plenary, Hon‟ble Home Minister directed that a suitable memorial at Moirang, 
proposed by the Manipur Government, may be considered for support.  This would be finalized 
by NEC in consultation with CS Manipur.  Accordingly, Chief Secretary, Manipur has bee n 
asked to consider proposing a suitable memorial/redevelopment of existing structures in memory 
of Netaji Subhash Chandra Bose at Moirang, as soon as possible,  so that this can be completed 
by August 2022.

Region.  Total fund release to date for these projects is Rs. 44.59 crore. The approved projects 
for listed states are as under:

sanctioned in December, 2015.  Rs.24.57 crore has been released.  Approximately 80% of 
the project has been completed.  The project was scheduled to be completed by June, 
2018.  However, the project is delayed and it is likely to be completed within February, 
2021.

25.26 crore has been sanctioned in March, 2019.  Rs.13.49 cr has been released.  The 
project was scheduled to be completed in 24 months. Pilgrimage Tourist amenities at 
Molungkimong, noksen church mission compound, aizuto, cathedral of  kohima, mission 
compound, zunheboto, wayside amenities at vankhosung (wokha) are proposed to be 
developed. 
c. Meghalaya: Development of Pilgrimage Facilitation in Meghalaya:  An amount of Rs. 
29.32 crore has been sanctioned in May, 2020.  Rs.6.53 cr has been released.  The project 
was scheduled to be completed in 24 months. Pilgrimage Tourist amenities at nongsawlia 
presbyterian church, sohra, naritang shakti peeth, aitnar pool and charanthala kali temple 
are proposed to be developed.

6.11.15 The Committee takes note of the projects related to tourism promotion that is being 
implemented under the NESIDS Scheme and hopes that with concerted efforts to 
implement these projects, global high-end tourism will be developed and promoted in the 
NER.  The Committee appreciates the efforts made towards educational tourism which has 
contributed to socio economic development of the Region. The Committee, therefore, 
recommends that Ministry must step up efforts to explore educational tourism at differe nt 
levels and platforms so as to achieve its full potential with an aim to make education more  
accessible and to provide qualified Human Resource for Tourism in North Eastern Region. 
6.11.16 The Committee appreciates Swadesh Darshan and PRASAD Scheme and hopes 
that these will help in making North Eastern Region a preferred destination for visitor s 
and tourists. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Ministry must work out 
modalities to efficiently utilize the funds allocated for projects undertaken so as to achieve 
the broader objective of making North Eastern Region a high-end tourist destination. 
 
TRADE AND INDUSTRIAL DEVELOPMENT SECTOR 
 
6.12 North East Industrial Development Scheme (NEIDS). 
 
6.12.1 Union Cabinet has approved the North East Industrial Development Scheme (NEIDS), 
2017, with an outlay of Rs. 3000 crore, for industrial units in North Eastern Region, comprising 
States of Arunachal Pradesh, Assam, Manipur, Meghalaya, Mizoram, Nagaland, Sikkim and 
Tripura to boost industrialization in the region. 
 
6.12.2 The Committee sought to know the details and funds sanctioned under North East 
Industrial Development Scheme (NEIDS). The Ministry informed that North East Industrial 
Development Scheme (NEIDS), 2017 was notified on 12th April, 2018 and came into force w.e.f. 
1st April, 2017 for a period of five years. For the period 2019-20, a token amount of Rs. 1.00 
crore was allocated which was utilized. In FY 2020-21, the budgetary allocation of Rs. 100.00 
crore has been made at BE stage under NEIDS 2017. At present, 301 industrial units have been 
recommended by the State Nodal Officer of NE States for registration under NEIDS 2017, out of 
which 202 units have already been granted registration by the Government of India. The 
proposed investment by these 202 registered units under the Scheme is Rs. 1409.70 crore. To 
date, the State Level Committee of Government of Assam has recommended the reimbursement 
of claims amounting to Rs. 36.03 crore in respect of 45 industrial units of Assam who have 
started their commercial production and filed their claims under NEIDS 2017. Presently, the 
claims are under pre-scrutiny by the independent audit agency. 

81 
 
 
6.12.3 On being asked regarding the steps which have bee n taken for the benefit and 
promotion of MSME sector in view of COVID-19 Pandem ic and which were the worst 
affected MSMEs and what measures have been taken to  revive them. In response, the 
Ministry has informed the Committee that Post Covid-19 Pande mic, Government has taken a 
number of initiatives under Aatma Nirbhar Bharat Abhiyan to support the MSME Sector in the 
country. Some of them are: 
 
i)  Rs 20,000 crore Subordinate Debt for MSMEs. 
ii)  Rs. 3 lakh crore Collateral free Automatic Loans for businesses, including MSMEs. 
iii)  Rs. 50,000 crore equity infusion through MSME Fund. 
iv) Newly revised criteria for the classification of MSMEs. 
v)  New Registration of MSMEs through 'Udyam Registration' for Ease of Doing 
Business. 
vi)  No global tenders for procurement up to Rs. 200 crore, this will help MSME. 
 
6.12.4 The Ministry further informed that an online Portal “Champions” has been launched on 
1st June, 2020 by the Hon'ble Prime Minister. This covers many aspects of e-governance 
including grievance redressal and handholding of MSMEs. Through the portal, a total of 26,693 
grievances have been redressed up to 31st January, 2021. 
 
6.12.5 The Ministry further informed that projects worth Rs. 25 crore have also been sanctioned 
to the NER States for livelihood generation for returnee migrant workers due to COVID-19 
Pandemic.  
 
6.12.6 The Ministry has also informed that various sectors including the MSME sector have 
been affected temporarily by the nation-wide lockdown/Covid-19 Pandemic in the country. 
 
6.12.7 The Committee in order to know various measures undertaken to revi ve the tea 
industry in NE. In reply, the Ministry has informed th at the Government is promoting the 
development of the tea industry through Tea Board by way of regulations under the Tea Act, 
1953.  Tea Board is also implementing the "Tea Development and Promotion Scheme (TDPS)'' 
which includes activities for improving tea production, quality up-gradation, research and 
extension, promotion of exports and value addition through financial and technical assistance to 
growers and other stakeholders of the tea industry.  A total amount of Rs 153.11 crore was spent 
by the Tea Board from 2017-18 to January, 2021 for various developmental activities in NE 
Region.  
 
6.12.8   The Ministry also informed that in order to increase the export of tea from NER, various 
promotional activities were undertaken like promotional Campaigns for International Tea Day- 
May 2020 which included the Fine plucking Initiative and International Tea Day Special Edition 
e-Auction- May 2020. Virtual Tea Conclave, Virtual Session of the Tea Conclave on Global 
Market Linkages and Business Development and Virtual B2B Meets for North-East Tea Sector 
were organized in 2020.  As an initiative towards Agriculture Export Policy, cluster formation in 
the three identified districts of Assam viz, Tinsukia, Dibrugarh and Sivsagar has been initiated 
that will cover almost 30 Self Help Groups and around 68 FPOs. 

82 
 
 
6.12.9    The Committee was apprised that under technological initiatives mobile app Chai 
Sahyog has been launched. An alternative auction platform junction has been set up in Jorhat, 
Assam to promote e-commerce and the auction platform started functioning from 1 st June 2020. 
Small growers are provided with an ID card with Quick Response Code (QR code) recording 
duly their details of plantation, training, banking, etc. Total 1,30,406 small tea growers were 
recorded and 78,816 cards were issued. 521 numbers of tea manufacturing units were enrolled 
under the mobile app. Small growers were collectivized to bring them into the value chain. 
Almost 1622 numbers of workshops covering 68893 numbers of beneficiaries were conducted.  
 
6.12.10 The Committee notes the details of funds sanctioned under NEIDS and 
recommends that the funds should be used efficiently to boost industrialization in the 
Region. The Committee is pleased with the number of initiatives taken by the Government 
under Aatma Nirbhar Bharat Abhiyan to support the MSME Sector. The Committee 
appreciates the efforts made for the revival of the tea industry in the NER and 
recommends that necessary technical and financial help should be extended to tea growers 
and other stakeholders which would help in improving tea production and quality u p-
gradation. 
 
6.13 Act East Policy 
 
6.13.1 The Committee sought to know the major initiatives under „Act East Policy‟, especially 
with the neighboring countries, in particular for Bangladesh. The Ministry replied that 
Bangladesh & India are making concerted efforts to enhance transport and connectivity with 
each other and through Bangladesh with the North Eastern Region. The focus has been on the 
railway, road, coastal as well as riverine connectivity. At present, five out of the six pre-1965 rail 
links have been restored, namely, Petrapole (West Bengal)-Benapole (Bangladesh), Gede (West 
Bengal)-Darshana (Bangladesh), Radhikapur (West Bengal)-Biral (Bangladesh) and Singhba d 
(West Bengal)-Rohanpur(Bangladesh) and the latest one - the Chilahati (Bangladesh) – Haldibari 
(West Bengal) link. Work is undergoing on the Shahbazpur (Bangladesh) – Mahishashan 
(Assam) link. In addition, when the trade via land came to a halt in the wake of the COVID-19 
Pandemic, parcel and container train services were introduced in July 2020 to ensure the supply 
of essential commodities. The work on the new Akhaura (Bangladesh)-Agartala (Tripura) cross-
border railway link has continued to progress. 
 
6.13.2 When asked about the initiatives with respect to Myanmar, Ministry replied that India is 
undertaking major connectivity and infrastructure projects such as the Kaladan Multi-Modal 
Transit Transport Project and the India-Myanmar-Thailand Trilateral Highway. These initiatives  
once operationalized will enhance bilateral connectivity and trade and will bring benefits to the 
local communities residing on the two sides.

December 2017 at the cost of Rs. 1177 crores. The 
project commenced in May, 2018 and the scheduled date 
of completion is May, 2021.   
 
6.13.4 The Committee notes the status of major initiatives taken under ‗Act East Policy ‘ 
including the physical progress of Agartala-Akhaura new rail link project and 
recommends that the said project should be completed within stipulated time and without 
any cost overruns.  
 
6.13.5 The Committee is displeased with the physical progress of road construction work of 
the Kaladan Multi-Modal Transit Transport Project and takes note of the reasons for the 
delay in the implementation of the project. The Committee recommends that the r easons 
for the delay in the project should be addressed and necessary action be taken so as to clear 
roadblocks in the implementation of the project undertaken.  
 
6.13.6 The Committee has taken cognizance of the status of the India-Myanmar-Thailand 
Trilateral Highway and recommends that the reasons for the delay must be addressed soon 
and the project should be closely monitored. The Committee would like to be appr ised 
about the progress made in this project. 
 
6.13.7 The Committee notes that Rs. 6,193 crore has been allocated for railway projec ts in 
the North-Eastern States. The Committee appreciates the fund-utilization pattern of the 
railway projects in the North-Eastern States. The Committee, therefore, recom mends to 
complete the ongoing projects in stipulated time and without cost overruns.

6.15 Food Processing Sector  
 
6.15.1 The Committee sought to know the status of development in Food Processing Sector in 
NER during last two years. The Ministry has informed the Committee that in view of its 
immense potential due to favorable agro-climatic conditions and by default organic cultivation 
practiced by farming communities, Ministry of Food Processing Industries has been making 
efforts for development of Food Processing sector in North Eastern Region of India.   
 
6.15.2 The schematic Intervention by Ministry of Food Processing Industries for development of 
Food Processing Sector in NER is as follows- 
(a) Pradhan Mantri Kisan Samapada Yojana (PMKSY) : A Central Sector Umbrella 
Scheme –Pradhan Mantri Kisan Samapada Yojana(PMKSY) approved in May, 2017 with an 
allocation of Rs. 6,000 Crores for the period 2016-20 co terminus with the 14 th Finance 
Commission Cycle is being implemented in the country including NER. The period of the 
Scheme has been extended to Year 2020-21. An additional new Scheme "Operation Greens 
(OG)" was introduced in PMKSY in November, 2018 for integrated development of 
value/supply chain of tomato, onion and potato (TOP) crops in selected States on pilot basis with 
an outlay of Rs 500 Crores. Conscious efforts are being made to increase the number of projects 
from North Eastern region under the component Schemes of PMKSY. The guidelines of the 
Schemes provide various concessions to the projects located in North Eastern Region. Further, 
Ministry is regularly conducting virtual meetings with promoters/investors of NER to assess the 
implementation of sanctioned projects and to find out issues/problems being faced by them. 
Ministry also writes letters to State governments, If found necessary, to help 
promoters/Implementing Agencies in getting statutory approvals in a timely manner and to avoid 
unnecessary delay in implementation.
